# RFC 4301 - Security Architecture for the Internet Protocol

IPsec architecture. Every requirement listed for RFC 4301, the tests linked to it, and their recorded reviews. This summary is included in the automatic compliance check, ./le rfc check.

## Overview

### Positive

what Ze has

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Tested both ways | 53 | of 101 mandatory requirements | a positive test checks that Ze does what the requirement demands and a negative one checks that it refuses what the requirement forbids |
| One polarity plus reason | 12 | of 101 mandatory requirements | the requirement admits no counter-case, so one polarity plus a recorded reason supplies the complete test coverage required |
| One polarity, unexcused | 0 | of 101 mandatory requirements | one direction is tested, the other is neither tested nor excused, and nothing states which |
| Tests shown to detect a fault | 245 | 245 of 269 tests linked to requirements, 9 without a fault test and 0 with outdated fault tests | each test was observed to fail when the code it checks was deliberately broken. The test, its stated claim, and the relevant code are checked against the saved record for changes; the deliberate fault is not run again. Having both positive and negative tests verifies a requirement only after both tests have been shown to detect a fault |

### Negative

what Ze owes

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Partial test coverage | 12 | of 101 mandatory requirements | scoped tests exist; the remaining obligation is unmet or unverified, with zero whole-requirement credit |
| Declared gap or no test | 21 | of 101 mandatory requirements | the requirements the summary declares a gap for, which say what Ze does not do yet, plus the ones no test carries and no annotation excuses. The at-a-glance table lists them apart: declared gaps (at every level), and mandatory requirements with no test and no annotation |
| Audit verdicts | 64 | of 101 mandatory requirements reviewed | 20 weak, wrong or unimplemented, 19 no longer current. Each is named below under its own requirement id |

### Neutral

measures that are neither good news nor bad

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Out of scope | 0 | of 101 mandatory requirements | an obligation that does not bind Ze, counted once its evidence passes. A {not-applicable} annotation says it never bound; a {feature-declined} annotation says its condition is an optional feature Ze does not offer, and quotes the RFC sentence that makes it optional. An annotation whose evidence fails is not counted here: the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. This classification records applicability and forms part of the total accounted for by the cards on this page |
| Not applicable | 0 | of 101 mandatory requirements | a {not-applicable} annotation says the obligation does not bind Ze. No test is owed only once its evidence passes; one whose evidence fails stays owed, and the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. It remains in the total that these cards add up to |
| Met below Ze | 3 | of 101 mandatory requirements | a {lower-layer} annotation says a layer under Ze performs the behavior, on state Ze installs into that layer, and names the producer that installs it. The obligation binds Ze and stays in the MUST bar, outside Ze verified: it is OS verified once a tagged test checks that layer's behavior on the state Ze installs, with a recorded break, and an OS testing gap until then |
| Optional feature declined | 0 | of 101 mandatory requirements | a {feature-declined} annotation says the obligation is conditional on a feature the RFC makes optional and Ze does not offer, and it quotes the sentence that makes it optional. The condition is false, so nothing is owed once that quote passes its evidence check; one whose quote fails stays owed, and the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. It remains in the total that these cards add up to |
| Linux cannot do this | 0 | of 101 mandatory requirements | Linux cannot provide the primitive this requirement needs. The obligation still binds Ze and stays unmet, but as a limit of the kernel, not of Ze's code: once its limit entry is verified, the row is not owed (OS blocked) and is left out of the MUST bar; one whose entry fails stays owed, and the figures count it as not built. It remains in the total that these cards add up to and earns no test verification credit |

### Totals

what the cards above are counted against

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Mandatory requirements (MUST level) | 101 | of 105 requirements this summary lists, at all levels | The RFC's MUST, MUST NOT, SHALL, SHALL NOT and REQUIRED requirements included in the automatic checks: the compliance check (./le rfc check) holds Ze to each of them. This is the total being assessed; the counts above show the recorded status of those requirements. |

The 9 counts marked as a part above are the whole of the 101 mandatory requirements: they add up to 101. Tests shown to detect a fault counts individual tests rather than requirements, so its count is separate.

A color names what the measure MEANS, not how well Ze scores on it. Green is a good outcome at any value, red is a bad one, and neither a population nor a scope count is an outcome, so both take no color. The number under the label is what says how far Ze has got. An outlined card with no colored edge, under Totals, is a total the other cards are counted against, not a measure.

| Card | Tone here | Why that color |
|---|---|---|
| Out of scope | neutral | no color: an obligation that never bound Ze is neither an achievement nor a failure, and counting it either way would be a claim |
| Tested both ways | ok | green at every value: a test pair is the outcome this gate exists to produce, and the count under the label is what says how far Ze has got |
| One polarity plus reason | ok | green at every value: where no counter-case exists, one polarity IS the complete answer, and a recorded reason is what the gate demands beside it |
| One polarity, unexcused | ok | green at zero, RED above it: one test direction is missing without a recorded reason |
| Partial test coverage | bad | green at zero, RED above it: testing one clause does not verify the whole requirement |
| Declared gap or no test | bad | green at zero, RED above it: a binding obligation that nothing shows Ze meets is owed, whether or not a reason is stated |
| Not applicable | neutral | no color: an obligation that never bound Ze is neither an achievement nor a failure, and counting it either way would be a claim |
| Met below Ze | neutral | no color: an obligation met below Ze is neither a test Ze wrote nor work Ze owes, and the two green counts above are what says how much Ze's own tests verify |
| Optional feature declined | neutral | no color: an obligation whose condition Ze never meets is neither an achievement nor a failure. The absent FEATURE is disclosed on the RFC's own status row, as an implementation gap a later scope decision can revisit |
| Linux cannot do this | neutral | neutral at every value: an evidenced platform limit is neither test verification nor a scope exemption |
| Tests shown to detect a fault | ok | green at every value: these tests have been shown to detect a deliberate fault. This card counts individual tests separately, so its total cannot be added to the requirement counts above |
| Audit verdicts | bad | RED on the first weak, wrong or unimplemented verdict, amber while a verdict is no longer current or a mandatory requirement is unreviewed, green when every one is judged sound and current |
| Mandatory requirements (MUST level) | outlined | outlined, with no colored edge: it is the total the other cards are counted against, not a measure, and a larger one is neither good news nor bad |

## Automatic checks

Included in automatic checks: Security Architecture for IP (RFC 4301): native control-plane SPD/SAD projected to kernel XFRM. The extraction walk of 2026-09-21 read all 89 normative sites in `rfc/full/rfc4301.txt` and grew the checklist from 24 rows to 81. Later work added rows, and on 2026-11-19 the checklist holds 105 rows: 96 MUST, 6 MUST NOT, 2 SHOULD and 1 MAY. Each row carries its own annotation, or none.

The MUST bar shows mandatory requirements (MUST, MUST NOT, REQUIRED, SHALL and SHALL NOT) that Ze and its operating system must meet. It includes only RFCs whose requirement lists have been checked against the original text. Ze verified means a test has been shown to fail when the required behavior is deliberately broken. Behavior provided by the operating system or a library is OS verified when a test checks it using the settings Ze installs and has also been shown to detect a deliberate fault; until then, it is an OS testing gap. Tests awaiting verification counts requirements a tagged test covers that no deliberate fault has yet been shown to break and that nothing records as unmet. Exclusion unchecked counts requirements claimed not to apply to Ze whose evidence is still to be checked. Not assessed counts requirements no tagged test covers and that nothing records as unmet, so nothing yet says whether Ze meets them. Not built counts requirements the RFC summary or a test review says Ze does not meet, in full or in part. The overview bar adds optional requirements (MAY, NOT RECOMMENDED, OPTIONAL, RECOMMENDED, SHOULD and SHOULD NOT) and requirements that do not apply to Ze. An RFC marked requirements not reviewed is excluded from these figures because its requirement list has not been checked against the original text. Counts are loaded from the RFC data files.

Source: [data/rfc/rfc4301.json](../../../data/rfc/rfc4301.json).

## Requirements

| Requirement | Text | Level | Section | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RFC4301-4.1-1` | a) A host implementation of IPsec MUST support both transport and tunnel mode. (§4.1) | MUST | 4.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestChildSAInstallsInDataplane`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go#L177). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestIPsecSAPerDestinationWithOSPFSelector`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install_test.go#L246). **negative:** no negative test. **{single-polarity}:** ze projects both modes -- IKE child SAs install tunnel-mode ESP and OSPFv3 RFC 4552 installs transport-mode ESP/AH; a capability-presence MUST has no meaningful negative (internal/component/ike/engine/child.go:224, :253, internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go:413, :441) |
| `RFC4301-4.1-2` | A security gateway MUST support tunnel mode (§4.1) | MUST | 4.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestChildSAInstallsInDataplane`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go#L192). **negative:** no negative test. **{single-polarity}:** ze (a security gateway) installs tunnel-mode ESP for every IKE-negotiated peer SA and policy (internal/component/ike/engine/child.go:224, :253, :281, :295) |
| `RFC4301-4.1-3` | Aside from the two exceptions below, whenever either end of a security association is a security gateway, the SA MUST be tunnel mode. (§4.1) | MUST | 4.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestChildSAInstallsInDataplane`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go#L179). **negative:** no negative test. **{single-polarity}:** the IKE child-SA path hardcodes tunnel mode for every peer SA, so a peer SA can never be transport (internal/component/ike/engine/child.go:40, :224, :253) |
| `RFC4301-4.1-4` | IKE creates pairs of SAs, so for simplicity, we choose to require that both SAs in a pair be of the same mode, transport or tunnel. (§4.1) | MUST | 4.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestChildSAInstallsInDataplane`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go#L181). **negative:** no negative test. **{single-polarity}:** the inbound and outbound child SAs of a pair are both built with modeTunnel, so the pair is always same-mode (internal/component/ike/engine/child.go:224, :253) |
| `RFC4301-4.1-5` | To permit this, the IPsec implementation MUST permit establishment and maintenance of multiple SAs between a given sender and receiver, with the same selectors. (§4.1) | MUST | 4.1 | **positive:** no positive test. **negative:** no negative test. **{gap}:** two halves. On Linux, XFRM (linux:CONFIG_XFRM_USER) keys the SAD by SPI, so SAs with identical selectors can coexist, and internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::InstallSA installs each by its SPI, but no test shows ze's IKE keeps two Child SAs with the same selectors to one peer installed together; on the VPP backend (internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go::InstallSA) the behaviour is unverified |
| `RFC4301-4.2-1` | Note: A compliant implementation MUST NOT allow instantiation of an ESP SA that employs both NULL encryption and no integrity algorithm. (§4.2) | MUST NOT | 4.2 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestIPsecESPRequiresIntegrity`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/plugins/ospf/config_ipsec_test.go#L121). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4301ESPNullEncryptionWithIntegrityIsInstantiated`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_esp_null_linux_test.go#L17). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestIPsecESPRequiresIntegrity`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/plugins/ospf/config_ipsec_test.go#L102). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4301ESPNullEncryptionWithoutIntegrityIsRefused`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_esp_null_linux_test.go#L36). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4301VPPESPNullEncryptionWithoutIntegrityIsRefused`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_esp_null_vpp_test.go#L29) |
| `RFC4301-4.4.1-1` | An IPsec implementation MUST have at least one SPD, and it MAY support multiple SPDs, if appropriate for the context in which the IPsec implementation operates. | `RFC4301-4.4.1.1-1` | The following selector parameters MUST be supported by all IPsec implementations to facilitate control of SA granularity. (§4.4.1.1) | MUST | 4.4.1.1 | **positive:** no positive test. **negative:** no negative test. **{gap}:** Ze's selectors carry IPv4 and IPv6 prefixes, a next-layer protocol, ANY or one port per side, and an ICMP type and code (dataplane.SPParams UpperProto, SrcPort and DstPort; an operator SPD entry takes one type and a code range, ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseICMPSelector). Missing: an address range that is not a prefix in the configuration (each entry takes one CIDR prefix; only a received selector is tiled into prefixes, engine/ts_narrow.go::rangePrefixes); a port range (ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseSPDPort accepts ANY or one port, and ipsec/traffic_selector.go::parsePortSelector ANY, OPAQUE or one port, the OPAQUE form then refused at commit by ipsec/traffic_selector.go::checkPortProgrammable); OPAQUE ports and the IPv6 OPAQUE next-layer protocol (RFC4301-4.4.1.1-2); exact Mobility Header type selectors: ipsec/traffic_selector.go::ValidForProtocol preserves the RFC-valid high-octet value, but ProgrammableForProtocol refuses constrained MH because Linux 7.3 net/xfrm/xfrm_policy.c::decode_session6 does not fill the received Type read by include/net/xfrm.h::xfrm_flowi_sport. The actual Child/backend guest probe delivered ANY Types 5/7 on local IN and routed FWD, while an exact Type 5 policy dropped matching Type 5 on both paths. Config, negotiation and Child installation now refuse exact MH before state changes; ANY remains usable. The wanted receive-flow extraction feature is plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-ipv6-mh-selector.md; and the Name selector (RFC4301-4.4.1.1-5) |

| `RFC4301-5.2-1` | Since the SPD-I is just a part of the SPD, if a packet that is looked up in the SPD-I cannot be matched to an entry there, then the packet MUST be discarded. (§4.4.1) | MUST | 4.4.1 | **positive:** no positive test. **negative:** no negative test. **{gap}:** re-examined under RULINGS R28 and narrowed by the owner decision of 2026-10-20. Where the catch-all is installed (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go, ranked last at dataplane.PriorityUnmatched) every inbound packet meets an SPD-I entry and the action is the RFC4301-4.4.1-3 choice. Three paths remain where an inbound packet can meet no SPD-I entry and the dataplane delivers it. The VPP backend is the first: it holds no entry bound to no interface (vppPolicyInterface; vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go), so it runs only under `unmatched bypass` (the default discard is refused at verify there) and installs no catch-all. OSPFv3 authentication with RFC 4552 manual keys is the second: the OSPF plugin installs its SAs and transport-mode SPD entries through whichever dataplane backend is active, loading the xfrm backend itself only when none is (defaultDataplaneSource, internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go), and the IKE engine neither installs nor releases those. On XFRM, while the IKE engine holds an applied configuration under `unmatched discard`, its if_id 0 catch-all (installUnmatched, ranked last) sits in the same SPD below them and is their final entry, read from the code and not yet tested; with no `vpn ipsec` block, after the engine's releaseSPD, under `unmatched bypass`, and on VPP, they are in force with no final DISCARD entry (owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md); a packet they do not match meets no SPD-I entry and the kernel delivers it. Whether that path gains a catch-all is an owner scope decision not yet taken. The daemon's start is the third, by owner ruling 5c of 2026-11-20: a start whose catch-all the dataplane refuses is not refused, but installs every catch-all entry the dataplane accepts, logs the refusal at Error under doctor-ipsec-spd-catch-all-missing, records no discard in force and has the ipsec-spd-install doctor check report the catch-all NOT in force with `ze signal restart` as the fix (assertUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go; installUnmatchedAtStart, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go; TestUnmatchedInstallFailureAtStartIsReportedByDoctor), so until a restart or a `vpn ipsec` commit installs it, an inbound packet no other entry matches meets no SPD-I entry in a direction whose entry was refused and the kernel delivers it; a bound XFRM interface whose own final entry the start could not install is reported the same way and gets none of its if_id 0 exemptions (recordInterfaceCatchAllAtStart, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureAtStartIsReportedByDoctor). Everywhere else a catch-all that cannot be installed fails the reload under either disposition, a platform without XFRM included (TestUnmatchedInstallFailureFailsTheReload, TestUnmatchedWithNoEnforcingDataplaneIsAnError), a start with no dataplane loaded is refused (TestUnmatchedWithNoDataplaneRefusesTheStart), a reload whose bound XFRM interface's own final entry at its if_id is refused fails too and keeps that interface's if_id 0 exemptions out (installXFRMInterfaceEntries, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureFailsTheApply), and `unmatched discard` is refused at verify where the dataplane says ahead of time it cannot hold the entry (verifyUnmatchedEnforceable). This window statement is about the IKE engine's SPD alone, and the OSPFv3 entries above are outside it. Outside the window from the first configuration apply to the IKE engine's exit, the IKE engine holds no SPD entry and no SA: applyConfig (internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go) installs the catch-all before any other SPD entry (Ze's own IKE is exempt on its sockets, bypassIKESocket, not by an SPD entry), and on every exit shutdown stops each peer (removing its Child SA), closes the IKE sockets, then releaseSPD removes the catch-all last, and keeps it installed with a warning when any other removal failed (warnSPDLeft, spd_left.go). So in those windows the IKE engine is not acting as an IPsec implementation and no packet is looked up in an SPD-I of its own, a reading the owner accepted on 2026-10-20 (TestRunInstallsNoSPDEntryBeforeTheFirstApply, TestRFC4301FinalEntryIsUnderEveryZeSPDEntry, TestReleaseSPDKeepsTheCatchAllWhenARemovalFails). TestRFC4301InboundClearPacketMatchingNoEntryFollowsTheUnmatchedLeaf shows the installed catch-all deciding an inbound clear packet on XFRM |

internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane now constructs both complete directional scopes before publishing either state, through internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope and dataplane.NewSAStateScope. Native positive and negative records prove single-pair whole and outer-fragmented bare ESP on an ESP-in-UDP state under the production SPD, including the state's selector rejection and a separate explicit SPD DISCARD. Those records do not establish general multi-pair or overlapping per-SA isolation. checkXFRMStateScopeCapabilities admits a state scope only on a receipt carrying feature bits 0 to 3 and 5 to 7, the ICMP reverse check, the forwarded ICMP error mapping and the forwarded ICMP error past a transit gate (required features 0xef); the carried K3 fragment guard is enrolled, a kernel whose receipt lacks bit 7, the runtime kernels qemu-1562c4ae and docker-1562c4ae included, is refused at Child SA install, and the bit-7 kernel docker-53c57c37 booted on arm64 on 2026-11-20 and ran the forwarded ICMP and fragment packet tests. Bounded scope, fragment and port-policy packet runs have passed, including a private whole-packet sibling-port cut that triggered the selected test's four own assertions. These observations do not complete the general isolation proof or renew its RFC records: the constrained permitted fragment and context cases now pass on the normal Ze kernel (eight selected packet/helper tests, including all 16 context rows), while separate broader RFC kernel-discrimination debt remains, with no private fault authorization or new C-enforcement credit. General isolation remains owned by plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md (AC-5, AC-7); ipsec-dual-form-receive-spd-bypass AC-4 now accepts the required normal Ze-kernel positive, negative and control packet tests for product closure. That acceptance does not remove this partial annotation or grant whole-requirement proof; the remaining RFC discrimination debt stays separate under the existing evidence rules. **Partial test coverage:** Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_state_scope_linux.go::checkXFRMStateScopeCapabilities, internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the annotation's home moved from plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md to plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md (AC-5, AC-7), whose AC-7 homes this row by name; no tagged unit changed. RFC 4301 Section 5.2: "Then match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry to verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received." Tested: "match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry". Gap: "verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received". Producer: internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane, with internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope and internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_state_scope_linux.go::checkXFRMStateScopeCapabilities. The tunnel and transport SAD-selector tests deliver an in-selector packet and drop source, port and protocol misses at the state selector (XfrmInStateMismatch +1); the boundary units pin direction, exact port and template and refuse an out-of-mask port; the transport state selector is the negotiated pair; bare ESP whole and fragmented is policy-checked on an ESP-in-UDP state. All fixtures are single-pair, so per-SA isolation within a multi-pair or sibling set stays unproven (AC-7). Scoped evidence only; zero whole-requirement credit. |

gap "Every SPD"; the final DISCARD entry is installed and tested only in the XFRM SPDs the IKE engine owns: the node-wide SPD at if_id 0 (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go) and the SPD of each XFRM interface a peer binds with vti bind (interfaceSPDCatchAll, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go). Two kinds of SPD carry no final entry. The VPP SPDs: internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go::vppPolicyInterface refuses a policy bound to no interface and vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, so installUnmatched installs nothing on VPP and no SPD that vppBackend.ensureSPD creates and binds receives a final entry. The kernel SPD holding the OSPFv3 RFC 4552 manual-key entries: internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go::defaultDataplaneSource hands the OSPF installer whichever backend is loaded, and buildIPsecPolicies installs per-interface protocol 89 transport entries with no final entry beneath them, in force with no vpn ipsec block and after the IKE engine releases its SPD. Owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md. **Partial test coverage:** Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go::applyConfig, internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go::releaseSPD, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go::installUnmatched moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after 51799ad534 changed applyConfig, installUnmatched and installXFRMInterfaceEntries; no tagged unit changed. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1: "Every SPD SHOULD have a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it." Tested: "a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it". Gap: "Every SPD". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go::vppPolicyInterface. Producer change read: under owner ruling 5c a catch-all the dataplane refuses at the daemon's start no longer refuses the IPsec block (assertUnmatched, installUnmatchedAtStart; recordInterfaceCatchAllAtStart for an interface SPD): the start keeps every entry the dataplane accepted, logs at Error under doctor-ipsec-spd-catch-all-missing and reports it through ipsec-spd-install, while a reload still refuses. The successful install the tagged units observe is unchanged: the catch-all still goes first on the first apply and is replayed beneath every Ze write, an unmatched clear packet is discarded in the kernel at if_id 0 and on a bound XFRM interface, and a refused interface catch-all fails the reload. The annotation still holds: the final entry exists only in the XFRM SPDs the engine owns, none on VPP SPDs or beneath the OSPF protocol 89 entries. A start-time install refusal is a further window without the final entry, reported rather than prevented, which RFC4301-5.2-1 and 5.2-9 must name. Scoped evidence only; zero whole-requirement credit. |
| `RFC4301-7-2` | Implementations SHOULD use the approach described in the Path MTU Discovery document (RFC 1191 [MD90], Section 6.3), which suggests periodically resetting the PMTU to the first-hop data-link MTU and then letting the normal PMTU Discovery processes update the PMTU as necessary. | `RFC4301-4.4.1-13` | Note that when an SPD entry is decorrelated all the resulting entries MUST be linked together, so that all members of the group derived from an individual, SPD entry (prior to decorrelation) can all be placed into caches and into the SAD at the same time. (§4.4.1) | MUST | 4.4.1 | **positive:** no positive test. **negative:** no negative test. **{gap}:** no owner decision declines decorrelation. Section 4.4.1 states "This RFC does not require a compliant implementation to make use of decorrelation.", and ze keeps the SPD ordered and never decorrelates it: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams writes each SPD entry as one kernel XFRM policy carrying its priority, and the kernel performs the ordered search, so no decorrelated group exists to link. The obligation stays declared until a scope decision of Thomas's declines decorrelation or ze decorrelates its SPD |
| `RFC4301-4.4.1.1-2` | Note that the Local and Remote ports may not be available in the case of receipt of a fragmented packet or if the port fields have been protected by IPsec (encrypted); thus, a value of OPAQUE also MUST be supported. (§4.4.1.1) | MUST | 4.4.1.1 | **positive:** no positive test. **negative:** no negative test. **{gap}:** the operator SPD list refuses OPAQUE and Ze maps OPAQUE onto no kernel selector yet, although the carried netlink patch now writes explicit masks so exactly port 0 is expressible (Linux limitation LL-NETLINK-XFRM-PORT-MASK, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, which records the library limit as carried by that patch). The ICMP type and code selector meets the same case, since a non-initial ICMP fragment carries neither and Linux classifies it as type 0 code 0: an operator entry whose ICMP selector holds type 0 code 0 is refused in direction out or both (internal/component/ike/ipsec/spd_policy.go::validateICMPFragments) and gets no forward projection in direction in (internal/component/ike/engine/spd_policy.go::spdEntryHasTransit), proven only by untagged unit tests, and no packet test shows such a fragment decided by an ANY entry and never by a typed one, which AC-9 of plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md owns; plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-opaque-selector-port-mask.md |
| `RFC4301-4.4.1.1-3` | If the SA requires a port value other than ANY or OPAQUE, an arriving fragment without ports MUST be discarded (§4.4.1.1) | MUST | 4.4.1.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryCarriesSelectorDirectionOrderAndTemplate`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_boundary_linux_test.go#L139). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryIsRefusedRatherThanWidened`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_boundary_linux_test.go#L200). **{partial}:** tested "If the SA requires a port value other than ANY or OPAQUE"; gap "an arriving fragment without ports MUST be discarded"; the tags prove that the entry Ze hands the kernel carries the exact port and that a port with a bit outside its mask is refused rather than widened (internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams). The discard of a fragment arriving on the SA is the kernel's state-scope check over the scope internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope installs with non-initial-fragment permission false (gokrazy/kernel/patches/0003-xfrm-state-scope.patch); no tagged test sends a fragment without ports on such an SA, nor asserts that installed permission, and the sibling and multi-pair probes (TestRFC4301SiblingSAsKeepInboundSelectorsBound, TestRFC4301MultipairSAsRejectSiblingScope) are untagged and unobserved; plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md. **Partial test coverage:** Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the annotation's home moved from plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md to plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md, whose AC-2 names this row; no tagged unit changed. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1.1: "If the SA requires a port value other than ANY or OPAQUE, an arriving fragment without ports MUST be discarded". Tested: "If the SA requires a port value other than ANY or OPAQUE". Gap: "an arriving fragment without ports MUST be discarded". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams, with internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope for the gap. TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryCarriesSelectorDirectionOrderAndTemplate keeps the exact inbound port 443 and TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryIsRefusedRatherThanWidened refuses a port with a bit outside its mask with no entry built, both with mutant records on xfrmSelectorPort: the required port reaches the kernel unwidened, the clause's precondition. childStateScope still installs non-initial-fragment permission false; no tagged test sends a fragment without ports on an exact-port SA under that permission, so the discard is unobserved, as AC-2 of the new home requires it be. Scoped evidence only; zero whole-requirement credit. |

| `RFC4301-4.4.2.1-16` | Bypass DSCP (T/F) or map to unprotected DSCP values (array) if needed to restrict bypass of DSCP values -- applicable to tunnel mode SAs. (§4.4.2.1) | MUST | 4.4.2.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMStateCarriesTheOuterDSCP`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_dscp_linux_test.go#L19). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMStateRefusesOuterDSCPItCannotWrite`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_dscp_linux_test.go#L45). **{partial}:** tested "Bypass DSCP (T/F)"; gap "map to unprotected DSCP values (array)"; the dscp-map leaf of a site-to-site peer sets the tunnel-mode SA's bypass-DSCP choice (copy is bypass true, a fixed value is bypass false and maps every inner DSCP to that one value), and the tags prove copy and a mapping to 0 reach the XFRM state and that the choice is refused on a transport-mode SA; no SAD entry holds a per-value map from inner to outer DSCP values, and on Linux XFRM internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDSCPFlag refuses a fixed value other than 0 (LL-XFRM-OUTER-DF-SET-DSCP, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md); plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md. **Partial test coverage:** Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. First verdict 2026-11-20 by an independent agent. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.2.1: "Bypass DSCP (T/F) or map to unprotected DSCP values (array) if needed to restrict bypass of DSCP values -- applicable to tunnel mode SAs." Tested: "Bypass DSCP (T/F)". Gap: "map to unprotected DSCP values (array)". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDSCPFlag. TestXFRMStateCarriesTheOuterDSCP asserts copy builds the outbound tunnel state without DontEncapDSCP and a mapping to 0 builds it with the flag (Linux then writes outer DSCP 0), so the state holds the bypass choice exactly. TestXFRMStateRefusesOuterDSCPItCannotWrite asserts a mapping on a transport-mode SA, outside the field's tunnel-mode applicability, is refused with ErrSAOptionRefused (its 46 and inbound cases serve other rows). The scoped assertions hold. The per-value array is honestly disclosed as unbuilt, and a fixed value other than 0 is refused on Linux XFRM by xfrmDSCPFlag (LL-XFRM-OUTER-DF-SET-DSCP), homed in plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md, which lists this row. No whole-requirement credit. |

RFC 4301 Section 5.1.2.1: "If the packet will immediately enter a domain for which the DSCP value in the outer header is not appropriate, that value MUST be mapped to an appropriate value for the domain". Tested: "that value MUST be mapped". Gap: "to an appropriate value for the domain". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDSCPFlag. Positives: the dscp-map leaf parses copy or 0 to 63 per peer (TestDSCPMapIsConfigurablePerPeer); the engine carries the peer's mapping onto the outbound SA with the inbound kept copy (TestOuterHeaderDSCPReachesTheBackend) and across rekey (TestRekeyKeepsTheOuterHeaderTreatment); XFRM builds DontEncapDSCP for 0 and not for copy (TestXFRMStateCarriesTheOuterDSCP); VPP sends ENCAP_COPY_DSCP or Dscp 46 (TestVPPInstallSACarriesTheOuterDSCP); and on the wire an EF inner datagram leaves with DSCP 46 under copy and 0 under the mapping (TestXFRMOuterHeaderCarriesTheOuterDSCP), which asserts one header per send. Negatives: a value XFRM cannot write is refused at state build, at config verify (TestXFRMSAOptionsSupportedRefusesOuterDSCPItCannotWrite, TestVerifyPeerSAOptionsRefusesWhatTheBackendCannotApply, naming the peer) and at install without a degraded Child SA (TestChildSAOptionRefusalIsNotKeptDegraded). The scoped claim holds: the mapping is applied, never silently replaced. The gap (a fixed 1 to 63 on Linux XFRM, a per-class table) stays unmet and is never counted conformant. |

| `RFC4301-5.2-9` | (No SAs are created in response to receipt of a packet that requires IPsec protection; only BYPASS or DISCARD cache entries can be created this way.) If there is no match, discard the traffic. (§5.2) | MUST | 5.2 | **positive:** no positive test. **negative:** no negative test. **{gap}:** re-examined under RULINGS R28 and narrowed by the owner decision of 2026-10-20. Where the catch-all is installed (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go, ranked last at dataplane.PriorityUnmatched) every inbound packet meets an SPD-I entry and the action is the RFC4301-4.4.1-3 choice. Three paths remain where an inbound packet can meet no SPD-I entry and the dataplane delivers it. The VPP backend is the first: it holds no entry bound to no interface (vppPolicyInterface; vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go), so it runs only under `unmatched bypass` (the default discard is refused at verify there) and installs no catch-all. OSPFv3 authentication with RFC 4552 manual keys is the second: the OSPF plugin installs its SAs and transport-mode SPD entries through whichever dataplane backend is active, loading the xfrm backend itself only when none is (defaultDataplaneSource, internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go), and the IKE engine neither installs nor releases those. On XFRM, while the IKE engine holds an applied configuration under `unmatched discard`, its if_id 0 catch-all (installUnmatched, ranked last) sits in the same SPD below them and is their final entry, read from the code and not yet tested; with no `vpn ipsec` block, after the engine's releaseSPD, under `unmatched bypass`, and on VPP, they are in force with no final DISCARD entry (owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md); a packet they do not match meets no SPD-I entry and the kernel delivers it. Whether that path gains a catch-all is an owner scope decision not yet taken. The daemon's start is the third, by owner ruling 5c of 2026-11-20: a start whose catch-all the dataplane refuses is not refused, but installs every catch-all entry the dataplane accepts, logs the refusal at Error under doctor-ipsec-spd-catch-all-missing, records no discard in force and has the ipsec-spd-install doctor check report the catch-all NOT in force with `ze signal restart` as the fix (assertUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go; installUnmatchedAtStart, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go; TestUnmatchedInstallFailureAtStartIsReportedByDoctor), so until a restart or a `vpn ipsec` commit installs it, an inbound packet no other entry matches meets no SPD-I entry in a direction whose entry was refused and the kernel delivers it; a bound XFRM interface whose own final entry the start could not install is reported the same way and gets none of its if_id 0 exemptions (recordInterfaceCatchAllAtStart, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureAtStartIsReportedByDoctor). Everywhere else a catch-all that cannot be installed fails the reload under either disposition, a platform without XFRM included (TestUnmatchedInstallFailureFailsTheReload, TestUnmatchedWithNoEnforcingDataplaneIsAnError), a start with no dataplane loaded is refused (TestUnmatchedWithNoDataplaneRefusesTheStart), a reload whose bound XFRM interface's own final entry at its if_id is refused fails too and keeps that interface's if_id 0 exemptions out (installXFRMInterfaceEntries, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureFailsTheApply), and `unmatched discard` is refused at verify where the dataplane says ahead of time it cannot hold the entry (verifyUnmatchedEnforceable). This window statement is about the IKE engine's SPD alone, and the OSPFv3 entries above are outside it. Outside the window from the first configuration apply to the IKE engine's exit, the IKE engine holds no SPD entry and no SA: applyConfig (internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go) installs the catch-all before any other SPD entry (Ze's own IKE is exempt on its sockets, bypassIKESocket, not by an SPD entry), and on every exit shutdown stops each peer (removing its Child SA), closes the IKE sockets, then releaseSPD removes the catch-all last, and keeps it installed with a warning when any other removal failed (warnSPDLeft, spd_left.go). So in those windows the IKE engine is not acting as an IPsec implementation and no packet is looked up in an SPD-I of its own, a reading the owner accepted on 2026-10-20 (TestRunInstallsNoSPDEntryBeforeTheFirstApply, TestRFC4301FinalEntryIsUnderEveryZeSPDEntry, TestReleaseSPDKeepsTheCatchAllWhenARemovalFails). TestRFC4301InboundClearPacketMatchingNoEntryFollowsTheUnmatchedLeaf shows the installed catch-all deciding an inbound clear packet on XFRM |

(§6) | MUST | 6 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4301OperatorBypassForwardsTransitEchoInClear`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_icmp_return_integration_linux_test.go#L491). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPErrorUnderOperatorDiscardMapsToReturnSA`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_icmp_return_integration_linux_test.go#L408). **{partial}:** tested "MUST be explicitly accounted for using SPD entries"; gap "Disposition of non-error, ICMP messages"; the split is by case, not by clause: Tested covers a forwarded ICMPv4 Echo Request between a protected host and the outside, not addressed to Ze, which the catch-all discards while no operator entry names it, which an operator BYPASS for ICMP forwards in clear, and which an operator out DISCARD for ICMP drops at its OUT policy, the bypass decided by the forward decision and gates the operator entry's projection installs (internal/component/ike/engine/spd_policy.go::spdTransitPolicies); Gap is the transit ICMPv6 non-error message, to which the first paragraph of Section 6 applies the whole section, and which no packet test sends, because the namespace lab and packet harness of these tests is IPv4 only; plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md. **Partial test coverage:** Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/spd_policy.go::spdTransitPolicies moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the row gained a {partial} annotation, replacing this agent's earlier enforced verdict, which had left the ICMPv6 case as a residual. RFC 4301 Section 6: "Disposition of non-error, ICMP messages (that are not addressed to the IPsec implementation itself) MUST be explicitly accounted for using SPD entries." and "The discussion in this section applies to ICMPv6 as well as to ICMPv4." Tested: "MUST be explicitly accounted for using SPD entries"; Gap: "Disposition of non-error, ICMP messages", split by case: the transit ICMPv6 non-error message. Producer: internal/component/ike/engine/spd_policy.go::spdTransitPolicies, the first path::Symbol in the reason. It is the right producer for the bypass: it builds the FWD decision and the transit gates that carry a forwarded Echo, and the gate the negative's discard must out-rank. The tested scope is enforced. Positive, TestRFC4301OperatorBypassForwardsTransitEchoInClear: under `unmatched discard` with no Child SA, a transit Echo Request (not addressed to Ze) is first not forwarded in either direction while no operator entry names it; then a both-direction operator BYPASS for ICMP, accepted by verifySPDOrder, installed through installSPDPolicies and read back from the kernel (transitWantInstalled, which also requires a forward policy), forwards a second Echo in each direction, seen in clear on the egress link. The entry is the only change between the two observations. Negative, TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPErrorUnderOperatorDiscardMapsToReturnSA, Echo case: an operator out DISCARD for ICMP, with an in BYPASS whose OUT transit gate would otherwise carry the Echo in clear, gives XfrmOutPolBlock +1 and no copy at the peer, protected or in clear. The gap is honest: every case is IPv4, and AC-8 of plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md names RFC4301-6-1's ICMPv6 pair. Wording finding, not a scope defect: the reason says the first paragraph of Section 6 applies the section to ICMPv6, but that sentence is the second paragraph (the first ends "using SPD entries."); the 6.2-3, -4 and -5 reasons say the same. Record finding: every 6-1 record breaks spdTransitPolicies by a panic, which proves reachability only, and the discard test still holds a positive record for a polarity it no longer claims. |

RFC 4301 Section 6.2: "If no SA exists that would carry the outbound ICMP message in question, and if no SPD entry would allow carriage of this outbound ICMP error message, then an IPsec implementation MUST map the message to the SA that would carry the return traffic associated with the packet that triggered the ICMP error message." Tested: "MUST map the message to the SA that would carry the return traffic"; Gap: "If no SA exists that would carry the outbound ICMP message in question", split by case: the ICMPv6 error. Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams, the first path::Symbol in the reason and the right one: it sets XFRM_POLICY_ICMP on the outbound protect policy from SPParams.ICMPErrorReturn (set by engine/child.go childPairPolicy), the flag xfrm_lookup_with_ifid requires before an XFRM_LOOKUP_ICMP lookup on the reversed header (icmp_route_lookup, xfrm_out_fwd_icmp) may use the policy; the earlier producer, xfrmStateFromParams, set the inbound reverse-check flag of 6.2-4 and was wrong. The tested scope is enforced. TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPErrorsMapUnderDiscard: under `unmatched discard` with no operator entry, a forwarded Destination Unreachable that no SA carries (the Child's policies are UDP only) meets the OUT catch-all (XfrmOutPolBlock +1) and is decrypted at the outside peer from the Child SA's own SPI byte for byte, beside a UDP control through that SA, with no copy in clear; its negative, the same error quoting traffic no SA carries, is seen neither protected nor in clear ("If no such SA exists, no SA will be created"). TestRFC4301LocalGeneratedICMPUsesReturnSA covers an error Ze generates for an authenticated closed-port trigger (NoPorts +1), decrypted from the return SA; the decoder also asserts the installed return policy's ICMPErrorReturn flag, and its untagged sibling without the flag is the control. The condition is held by commit refusals (refuseICMPErrorBypass: TestICMPOverlapBypassCatchAll, TestICMPOverlapBypassEntryRefused, TestICMPOverlapBypassTransitGate) with accepted disjoint and unchecked controls (TestICMPOverlapBypassEntryAccepted), covering protocols 1 and 58. The gap is honest: Section 6 says "The discussion in this section applies to ICMPv6 as well as to ICMPv4", every packet case is ICMPv4, and AC-8 of plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md owns the ICMPv6 case. Record finding: the new packet-test records break their producers (appendChildOutboundForwardPolicy, spdCatchAllEntries, xfrmPolicyFromParams) by a panic, which proves reachability only; the overlap-gate records are real mutants. |

The tested scope is enforced, the violating input refused in both receiver cases: TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPUsesPayloadPolicy sends on a real Child SA an error whose reversed quote is traffic the SA does not carry, waits for the kernel's reject counter to rise by one, and asserts it never reaches the protected host while the UDP control after it does; the same error quoting the SA's traffic is forwarded, so the refusal is tied to the check. TestICMPErrorPayloadHeaderCheckedOnLocalDelivery gives the same pair for an error addressed to Ze, with a no-flag control. Label finding, not a test defect: the two tests name the polarities oppositely (the local-delivery test calls the refusal negative, the forwarded test calls it positive); each test carries both. They grant fragment permission (fragmentPermittedChild), so the selector check makes the discard. The boundary units pin the inbound selector and template. The gateway receiver (FWD after K3 gathers) has no tagged test, as the annotation says. Scoped evidence only; zero whole-requirement credit. |
| `RFC4301-8.1-1` | All IPsec implementations MUST support the option of copying the DF bit from an outbound packet to the tunnel mode header that it emits, when traffic is carried via a tunnel mode SA (§8.1) | MUST | 8.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestVPPInstallSACarriesTheDFTreatment`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_df_vpp_test.go#L22). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMOuterHeaderCarriesTheDFTreatment`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_outer_header_integration_linux_test.go#L208). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMStateCarriesTheDFTreatment`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_df_linux_test.go#L40). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMStateCarriesTheDFTreatment`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_df_linux_test.go#L41) |
| `RFC4301-8.1-2` | This means that it MUST be possible to configure the implementation's treatment of the DF bit (set, clear, copy from inner header) for each SA. (§8.1) | MUST | 8.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestDFBitIsConfigurablePerPeer`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/ipsec/rfc4301_df_bit_test.go#L36). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestDFBitTreatmentReachesTheBackend`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_df_bit_test.go#L19). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestRekeyKeepsTheOuterHeaderTreatment`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_outer_header_rekey_test.go#L19). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestVPPInstallSACarriesTheDFTreatment`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_df_vpp_test.go#L23). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMOuterHeaderCarriesTheDFTreatment`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_outer_header_integration_linux_test.go#L209). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMStateCarriesTheDFTreatment`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_df_linux_test.go#L42). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestDFBitValidation`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/ipsec/rfc4301_df_bit_test.go#L64). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestVPPInstallSARefusesDFItCannotExpress`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_df_vpp_test.go#L56). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMStateRefusesDFItCannotExpress`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_df_linux_test.go#L66). **{partial}:** tested "clear, copy from inner header"; gap "set"; copy and clear are configurable per peer (df-bit), and the tags prove each reaches the SA on Linux XFRM and VPP and the wire, and that a treatment a dataplane cannot apply is refused at commit and again at install rather than installed as another; Gap is DF set, which internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDFFlag and internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go::vppDFError refuse because neither the XFRM state nor VPP's ESP encrypt path writes a set DF bit on an ESP tunnel header (LL-XFRM-OUTER-DF-SET-DSCP, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint and not a kernel limit: an nftables rewrite of the outer header could write it); the owner decided that refusal is the behaviour for the first release, with a Ze kernel patch after it (plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md), and it is never counted conformant; plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md. **Partial test coverage:** Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. First verdict 2026-11-20 by an independent agent, after the annotation was re-homed to plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md (which lists this row); no tagged unit changed. RFC 4301 Section 8.1: "This means that it MUST be possible to configure the implementation's treatment of the DF bit (set, clear, copy from inner header) for each SA." Tested: "clear, copy from inner header". Gap: "set". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDFFlag and internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go::vppDFError. Positives: df-bit parses per peer with absent as copy (TestDFBitIsConfigurablePerPeer); the engine hands the outbound SA the peer's treatment and the inbound copy, exactly one of each (TestDFBitTreatmentReachesTheBackend), and rekey keeps it (TestRekeyKeepsTheOuterHeaderTreatment); XFRM sets NoPMTUDisc for clear and not for copy (TestXFRMStateCarriesTheDFTreatment); VPP sends ENCAP_COPY_DF for copy and neither DF flag for clear (TestVPPInstallSACarriesTheDFTreatment); on the wire clear emits a clear outer DF for a set inner bit and copy follows the inner bit (TestXFRMOuterHeaderCarriesTheDFTreatment). Negatives: set, and a treatment on a transport, inbound or IPv6-outer SA, are refused with ErrSAOptionRefused on XFRM and VPP with nothing installed or sent (TestXFRMStateRefusesDFItCannotExpress, TestVPPInstallSARefusesDFItCannotExpress), and ValidateOuterHeader refuses set and an inapplicable clear (TestDFBitValidation). The scoped claim holds; DF set is refused, never installed as another treatment, and stays unmet. |
| `RFC4301-8.2.1-1` | When such traffic arrives, if the traffic would exceed the updated PMTU value the traffic MUST be handled as follows: Case 1: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv4 and has the DF bit set. The implementation SHOULD discard the packet and send a PMTU ICMP message. Case 2: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv4 and has the DF bit clear. The implementation SHOULD fragment (before or after encryption per its configuration) and then forward the fragments. It SHOULD NOT send a PMTU ICMP message. Case 3: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv6. The implementation SHOULD discard the packet and send a PMTU ICMP message. (§8.2.1) | MUST | 8.2.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMPolicyPMTUDiscardsAndSignalsOverAnIPv6OuterSA`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L170). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMPolicyPMTUDiscardsAndSignalsOverTheSAPMTU`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L159). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMTunnelPMTUDiscardsAndSignalsOverAnIPv6OuterSA`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L146). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMTunnelPMTUDiscardsAndSignalsOverTheSAPMTU`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L133). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMPolicyPMTUFragmentsADFClearPacket`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L238). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMTunnelPMTUFragmentsADFClearPacket`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L227) |
| `RFC4301-8.2.2-1` | In all IPsec implementations, the PMTU associated with an SA MUST be "aged" and some mechanism is required to update the PMTU in a timely manner (§8.2.2) | MUST | 8.2.2 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMPolicyPMTUAgesBackToTheLinkMTU`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L291). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMTunnelPMTUAgesBackToTheLinkMTU`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L280). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMTunnelPMTUAgesBackToTheLinkMTUOverIPv6`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_ipv6_integration_linux_test.go#L34). **negative:** no negative test. **{lower-layer}:** Linux route PMTU exceptions aged by net.ipv4.route.mtu_expires and net.ipv6.route.mtu_expires (__ip_rt_update_pmtu in net/ipv4/route.c, rt6_do_update_pmtu in net/ipv6/route.c) under the XFRM bundle that derives the SA inner MTU (xfrm_bundle_ok in net/xfrm/xfrm_policy.c), linux:CONFIG_XFRM_USER; under the default df-bit copy, internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmNoPMTUDisc leaves the outer DF bit copied on the state Ze installs, so a router's Fragmentation Needed or Packet Too Big about the SA's ESP packets lowers the outer path PMTU and the kernel ages it; under df-bit clear the state carries XFRM_STATE_NOPMTUDISC (which the kernel also sets on an IPv4 state not flagged inbound whenever net.ipv4.ip_no_pmtu_disc is set, __xfrm_init_state in net/xfrm/xfrm_state.c), xfrm4_tunnel_encap_add writes the IPv4 outer DF clear, routers fragment the ESP packets and return nothing, so no PMTU is learned from them, and the SA's PMTU is the outer route's MTU, the first-hop link's or one other traffic to the same endpoint learned, aged the same way; the period is the operator's `sysctl setting net.ipv4.route.mtu_expires` or `net.ipv6.route.mtu_expires` |

## Gaps and untested MUSTs

| Requirement | State | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| [`RFC4301-4.1-5`](#rfc4301-4.1-5) To permit this, the IPsec implementation MUST permit establishment and maintenance of multiple SAs between a given sender and receiver, with the same selectors. (§4.1) | {gap}, no test | two halves. On Linux, XFRM (linux:CONFIG_XFRM_USER) keys the SAD by SPI, so SAs with identical selectors can coexist, and internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::InstallSA installs each by its SPI, but no test shows ze's IKE keeps two Child SAs with the same selectors to one peer installed together; on the VPP backend (internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go::InstallSA) the behaviour is unverified |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1-2`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-2) The SPD, or relevant caches, must be consulted during the processing of all traffic (inbound and outbound), including traffic not protected by IPsec, that traverses the IPsec boundary. This includes IPsec management traffic such as IKE. (§4.4.1) | {gap}, no test | two halves. On Linux, XFRM consults the SPD ze installs (internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::InstallPolicy, including the forward policy RequiresForwardPolicy asks for) on every packet crossing the boundary, but no test reads the installed policies for traffic ze does not protect, IKE included; on the VPP backend (internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go) SPD consultation of all traffic is unverified |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1.1-1`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.1-1) The following selector parameters MUST be supported by all IPsec implementations to facilitate control of SA granularity. (§4.4.1.1) | {gap}, no test | Ze's selectors carry IPv4 and IPv6 prefixes, a next-layer protocol, ANY or one port per side, and an ICMP type and code (dataplane.SPParams UpperProto, SrcPort and DstPort; an operator SPD entry takes one type and a code range, ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseICMPSelector). Missing: an address range that is not a prefix in the configuration (each entry takes one CIDR prefix; only a received selector is tiled into prefixes, engine/ts_narrow.go::rangePrefixes); a port range (ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseSPDPort accepts ANY or one port, and ipsec/traffic_selector.go::parsePortSelector ANY, OPAQUE or one port, the OPAQUE form then refused at commit by ipsec/traffic_selector.go::checkPortProgrammable); OPAQUE ports and the IPv6 OPAQUE next-layer protocol (RFC4301-4.4.1.1-2); exact Mobility Header type selectors: ipsec/traffic_selector.go::ValidForProtocol preserves the RFC-valid high-octet value, but ProgrammableForProtocol refuses constrained MH because Linux 7.3 net/xfrm/xfrm_policy.c::decode_session6 does not fill the received Type read by include/net/xfrm.h::xfrm_flowi_sport. The actual Child/backend guest probe delivered ANY Types 5/7 on local IN and routed FWD, while an exact Type 5 policy dropped matching Type 5 on both paths. Config, negotiation and Child installation now refuse exact MH before state changes; ANY remains usable. The wanted receive-flow extraction feature is plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-ipv6-mh-selector.md; and the Name selector (RFC4301-4.4.1.1-5) |
| [`RFC4301-5.2-1`](#rfc4301-5.2-1) Since the SPD-I is just a part of the SPD, if a packet that is looked up in the SPD-I cannot be matched to an entry there, then the packet MUST be discarded. (§4.4.1) | {gap}, no test | re-examined under RULINGS R28 and narrowed by the owner decision of 2026-10-20. Where the catch-all is installed (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go, ranked last at dataplane.PriorityUnmatched) every inbound packet meets an SPD-I entry and the action is the RFC4301-4.4.1-3 choice. Three paths remain where an inbound packet can meet no SPD-I entry and the dataplane delivers it. The VPP backend is the first: it holds no entry bound to no interface (vppPolicyInterface; vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go), so it runs only under `unmatched bypass` (the default discard is refused at verify there) and installs no catch-all. OSPFv3 authentication with RFC 4552 manual keys is the second: the OSPF plugin installs its SAs and transport-mode SPD entries through whichever dataplane backend is active, loading the xfrm backend itself only when none is (defaultDataplaneSource, internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go), and the IKE engine neither installs nor releases those. On XFRM, while the IKE engine holds an applied configuration under `unmatched discard`, its if_id 0 catch-all (installUnmatched, ranked last) sits in the same SPD below them and is their final entry, read from the code and not yet tested; with no `vpn ipsec` block, after the engine's releaseSPD, under `unmatched bypass`, and on VPP, they are in force with no final DISCARD entry (owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md); a packet they do not match meets no SPD-I entry and the kernel delivers it. Whether that path gains a catch-all is an owner scope decision not yet taken. The daemon's start is the third, by owner ruling 5c of 2026-11-20: a start whose catch-all the dataplane refuses is not refused, but installs every catch-all entry the dataplane accepts, logs the refusal at Error under doctor-ipsec-spd-catch-all-missing, records no discard in force and has the ipsec-spd-install doctor check report the catch-all NOT in force with `ze signal restart` as the fix (assertUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go; installUnmatchedAtStart, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go; TestUnmatchedInstallFailureAtStartIsReportedByDoctor), so until a restart or a `vpn ipsec` commit installs it, an inbound packet no other entry matches meets no SPD-I entry in a direction whose entry was refused and the kernel delivers it; a bound XFRM interface whose own final entry the start could not install is reported the same way and gets none of its if_id 0 exemptions (recordInterfaceCatchAllAtStart, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureAtStartIsReportedByDoctor). Everywhere else a catch-all that cannot be installed fails the reload under either disposition, a platform without XFRM included (TestUnmatchedInstallFailureFailsTheReload, TestUnmatchedWithNoEnforcingDataplaneIsAnError), a start with no dataplane loaded is refused (TestUnmatchedWithNoDataplaneRefusesTheStart), a reload whose bound XFRM interface's own final entry at its if_id is refused fails too and keeps that interface's if_id 0 exemptions out (installXFRMInterfaceEntries, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureFailsTheApply), and `unmatched discard` is refused at verify where the dataplane says ahead of time it cannot hold the entry (verifyUnmatchedEnforceable). This window statement is about the IKE engine's SPD alone, and the OSPFv3 entries above are outside it. Outside the window from the first configuration apply to the IKE engine's exit, the IKE engine holds no SPD entry and no SA: applyConfig (internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go) installs the catch-all before any other SPD entry (Ze's own IKE is exempt on its sockets, bypassIKESocket, not by an SPD entry), and on every exit shutdown stops each peer (removing its Child SA), closes the IKE sockets, then releaseSPD removes the catch-all last, and keeps it installed with a warning when any other removal failed (warnSPDLeft, spd_left.go). So in those windows the IKE engine is not acting as an IPsec implementation and no packet is looked up in an SPD-I of its own, a reading the owner accepted on 2026-10-20 (TestRunInstallsNoSPDEntryBeforeTheFirstApply, TestRFC4301FinalEntryIsUnderEveryZeSPDEntry, TestReleaseSPDKeepsTheCatchAllWhenARemovalFails). TestRFC4301InboundClearPacketMatchingNoEntryFollowsTheUnmatchedLeaf shows the installed catch-all deciding an inbound clear packet on XFRM |
| [`RFC4301-5.2-2`](#rfc4301-5.2-2) Then match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry to verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received. (§5.2) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry"; gap "verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received"; internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane now constructs both complete directional scopes before publishing either state, through internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope and dataplane.NewSAStateScope. Native positive and negative records prove single-pair whole and outer-fragmented bare ESP on an ESP-in-UDP state under the production SPD, including the state's selector rejection and a separate explicit SPD DISCARD. Those records do not establish general multi-pair or overlapping per-SA isolation. checkXFRMStateScopeCapabilities admits a state scope only on a receipt carrying feature bits 0 to 3 and 5 to 7, the ICMP reverse check, the forwarded ICMP error mapping and the forwarded ICMP error past a transit gate (required features 0xef); the carried K3 fragment guard is enrolled, a kernel whose receipt lacks bit 7, the runtime kernels qemu-1562c4ae and docker-1562c4ae included, is refused at Child SA install, and the bit-7 kernel docker-53c57c37 booted on arm64 on 2026-11-20 and ran the forwarded ICMP and fragment packet tests. Bounded scope, fragment and port-policy packet runs have passed, including a private whole-packet sibling-port cut that triggered the selected test's four own assertions. These observations do not complete the general isolation proof or renew its RFC records: the constrained permitted fragment and context cases now pass on the normal Ze kernel (eight selected packet/helper tests, including all 16 context rows), while separate broader RFC kernel-discrimination debt remains, with no private fault authorization or new C-enforcement credit. General isolation remains owned by plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md (AC-5, AC-7); ipsec-dual-form-receive-spd-bypass AC-4 now accepts the required normal Ze-kernel positive, negative and control packet tests for product closure. That acceptance does not remove this partial annotation or grant whole-requirement proof; the remaining RFC discrimination debt stays separate under the existing evidence rules |
| [`RFC4301-7-1`](#rfc4301-7-1) Note: AH and ESP cannot be applied using transport mode to IPv4 packets that are fragments. Only tunnel mode can be employed in such cases. (§4.1) | {gap}, no test | ze's IKE negotiates transport-mode Child SAs for any address family (internal/component/ike/ipsec/traffic_selector.go::parseChildMode), so the rule is reachable over IPv4; on Linux, XFRM reassembles an inbound fragment before its policy check and applies ESP before outbound fragmentation, with no test over the state ze installs, and on the VPP backend (internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go) the behaviour is unverified |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1-3`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-3) Every SPD SHOULD have a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it. (§4.4.1) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it"; gap "Every SPD"; the final DISCARD entry is installed and tested only in the XFRM SPDs the IKE engine owns: the node-wide SPD at if_id 0 (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go) and the SPD of each XFRM interface a peer binds with vti bind (interfaceSPDCatchAll, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go). Two kinds of SPD carry no final entry. The VPP SPDs: internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go::vppPolicyInterface refuses a policy bound to no interface and vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, so installUnmatched installs nothing on VPP and no SPD that vppBackend.ensureSPD creates and binds receives a final entry. The kernel SPD holding the OSPFv3 RFC 4552 manual-key entries: internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go::defaultDataplaneSource hands the OSPF installer whichever backend is loaded, and buildIPsecPolicies installs per-interface protocol 89 transport entries with no final entry beneath them, in force with no vpn ipsec block and after the IKE engine releases its SPD. Owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md |
| [`RFC4301-4.1-10`](#rfc4301-4.1-10) In transport mode, the DSCP value might change en route, but this should not cause problems with respect to IPsec processing since the value is not employed for SA selection and MUST NOT be checked as part of SA/packet validation. (§4.1) | no test | no test carries this requirement id; annotated {lower-layer}: Linux XFRM SA and policy lookup (net/xfrm, the XFRM core linux:CONFIG_XFRM_USER selects); internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams writes no DSCP into the state and xfrmPolicyFromParams none into the selector, so no value Ze installs is read against DSCP |
| [`RFC4301-4.1-11`](#rfc4301-4.1-11) In IPv6, the security protocol header appears after the base IP header and selected extension headers, but may appear before or after destination options; it MUST appear before next layer protocols (e.g., TCP, UDP, Stream Control Transmission Protocol (SCTP)). (§4.1) | no test | no test carries this requirement id; annotated {lower-layer}: Linux XFRM ESP output (net/ipv6/esp6.c), linux:CONFIG_INET6_ESP; internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams installs the ESP state and the kernel output path builds every header, so no value Ze writes decides where the ESP header sits |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1-13`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-13) Note that when an SPD entry is decorrelated all the resulting entries MUST be linked together, so that all members of the group derived from an individual, SPD entry (prior to decorrelation) can all be placed into caches and into the SAD at the same time. (§4.4.1) | {gap}, no test | no owner decision declines decorrelation. Section 4.4.1 states "This RFC does not require a compliant implementation to make use of decorrelation.", and ze keeps the SPD ordered and never decorrelates it: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams writes each SPD entry as one kernel XFRM policy carrying its priority, and the kernel performs the ordered search, so no decorrelated group exists to link. The obligation stays declared until a scope decision of Thomas's declines decorrelation or ze decorrelates its SPD |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1.1-2`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.1-2) Note that the Local and Remote ports may not be available in the case of receipt of a fragmented packet or if the port fields have been protected by IPsec (encrypted); thus, a value of OPAQUE also MUST be supported. (§4.4.1.1) | {gap}, no test | the operator SPD list refuses OPAQUE and Ze maps OPAQUE onto no kernel selector yet, although the carried netlink patch now writes explicit masks so exactly port 0 is expressible (Linux limitation LL-NETLINK-XFRM-PORT-MASK, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, which records the library limit as carried by that patch). The ICMP type and code selector meets the same case, since a non-initial ICMP fragment carries neither and Linux classifies it as type 0 code 0: an operator entry whose ICMP selector holds type 0 code 0 is refused in direction out or both (internal/component/ike/ipsec/spd_policy.go::validateICMPFragments) and gets no forward projection in direction in (internal/component/ike/engine/spd_policy.go::spdEntryHasTransit), proven only by untagged unit tests, and no packet test shows such a fragment decided by an ANY entry and never by a typed one, which AC-9 of plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md owns; plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-opaque-selector-port-mask.md |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1.1-3`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.1-3) If the SA requires a port value other than ANY or OPAQUE, an arriving fragment without ports MUST be discarded (§4.4.1.1) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "If the SA requires a port value other than ANY or OPAQUE"; gap "an arriving fragment without ports MUST be discarded"; the tags prove that the entry Ze hands the kernel carries the exact port and that a port with a bit outside its mask is refused rather than widened (internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams). The discard of a fragment arriving on the SA is the kernel's state-scope check over the scope internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope installs with non-initial-fragment permission false (gokrazy/kernel/patches/0003-xfrm-state-scope.patch); no tagged test sends a fragment without ports on such an SA, nor asserts that installed permission, and the sibling and multi-pair probes (TestRFC4301SiblingSAsKeepInboundSelectorsBound, TestRFC4301MultipairSAsRejectSiblingScope) are untagged and unobserved; plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1.1-5`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.1-5) The name used to match this field is communicated during the IKE negotiation in the ID payload. In this context, the initiator's Source IP address (inner IP header in tunnel mode) is bound to the Remote IP address in the SAD entry created by the IKE negotiation. This address overrides the Remote IP address value in the SPD, when the SPD entry is selected in this fashion. All IPsec implementations MUST support this use of names. (§4.4.1.1) | {gap}, no test | no name-valued selector selects an operator SPD entry during IKE; plan/release/fixes/spec-ipsec-spd-name-selection.md |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1.2-2`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.2-2) Until IKE provides a facility that conveys the semantics that are expressed in the SPD via selector sets (as described below), users MUST NOT include multiple selector sets in a single SPD entry unless the access control intent aligns with the IKE "mix and match" semantics. (§4.4.1.2) | {gap}, no test | no owner decision declines multiple selector sets per SPD entry.
