| [`RFC4301-5.2-9`](#rfc4301-5.2-9) (No SAs are created in response to receipt of a packet that requires IPsec protection; only BYPASS or DISCARD cache entries can be created this way.) If there is no match, discard the traffic. (§5.2) | {gap}, no test | re-examined under RULINGS R28 and narrowed by the owner decision of 2026-10-20. Where the catch-all is installed (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go, ranked last at dataplane.PriorityUnmatched) every inbound packet meets an SPD-I entry and the action is the RFC4301-4.4.1-3 choice. Three paths remain where an inbound packet can meet no SPD-I entry and the dataplane delivers it. The VPP backend is the first: it holds no entry bound to no interface (vppPolicyInterface; vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go), so it runs only under `unmatched bypass` (the default discard is refused at verify there) and installs no catch-all. OSPFv3 authentication with RFC 4552 manual keys is the second: the OSPF plugin installs its SAs and transport-mode SPD entries through whichever dataplane backend is active, loading the xfrm backend itself only when none is (defaultDataplaneSource, internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go), and the IKE engine neither installs nor releases those. On XFRM, while the IKE engine holds an applied configuration under `unmatched discard`, its if_id 0 catch-all (installUnmatched, ranked last) sits in the same SPD below them and is their final entry, read from the code and not yet tested; with no `vpn ipsec` block, after the engine's releaseSPD, under `unmatched bypass`, and on VPP, they are in force with no final DISCARD entry (owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md); a packet they do not match meets no SPD-I entry and the kernel delivers it. Whether that path gains a catch-all is an owner scope decision not yet taken. The daemon's start is the third, by owner ruling 5c of 2026-11-20: a start whose catch-all the dataplane refuses is not refused, but installs every catch-all entry the dataplane accepts, logs the refusal at Error under doctor-ipsec-spd-catch-all-missing, records no discard in force and has the ipsec-spd-install doctor check report the catch-all NOT in force with `ze signal restart` as the fix (assertUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go; installUnmatchedAtStart, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go; TestUnmatchedInstallFailureAtStartIsReportedByDoctor), so until a restart or a `vpn ipsec` commit installs it, an inbound packet no other entry matches meets no SPD-I entry in a direction whose entry was refused and the kernel delivers it; a bound XFRM interface whose own final entry the start could not install is reported the same way and gets none of its if_id 0 exemptions (recordInterfaceCatchAllAtStart, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureAtStartIsReportedByDoctor). Everywhere else a catch-all that cannot be installed fails the reload under either disposition, a platform without XFRM included (TestUnmatchedInstallFailureFailsTheReload, TestUnmatchedWithNoEnforcingDataplaneIsAnError), a start with no dataplane loaded is refused (TestUnmatchedWithNoDataplaneRefusesTheStart), a reload whose bound XFRM interface's own final entry at its if_id is refused fails too and keeps that interface's if_id 0 exemptions out (installXFRMInterfaceEntries, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureFailsTheApply), and `unmatched discard` is refused at verify where the dataplane says ahead of time it cannot hold the entry (verifyUnmatchedEnforceable). This window statement is about the IKE engine's SPD alone, and the OSPFv3 entries above are outside it. Outside the window from the first configuration apply to the IKE engine's exit, the IKE engine holds no SPD entry and no SA: applyConfig (internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go) installs the catch-all before any other SPD entry (Ze's own IKE is exempt on its sockets, bypassIKESocket, not by an SPD entry), and on every exit shutdown stops each peer (removing its Child SA), closes the IKE sockets, then releaseSPD removes the catch-all last, and keeps it installed with a warning when any other removal failed (warnSPDLeft, spd_left.go). So in those windows the IKE engine is not acting as an IPsec implementation and no packet is looked up in an SPD-I of its own, a reading the owner accepted on 2026-10-20 (TestRunInstallsNoSPDEntryBeforeTheFirstApply, TestRFC4301FinalEntryIsUnderEveryZeSPDEntry, TestReleaseSPDKeepsTheCatchAllWhenARemovalFails). TestRFC4301InboundClearPacketMatchingNoEntryFollowsTheUnmatchedLeaf shows the installed catch-all deciding an inbound clear packet on XFRM |

## Test status

A test reads not verified when no record shows it failing after a deliberate fault. Until then, the test has not been shown to detect a fault in the required behavior.

### [`RFC4301-4.1-1`](#rfc4301-4.1-1)

a) A host implementation of IPsec MUST support both transport and tunnel mode. (§4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. IKE Child SA install asserts Mode==tunnel on both SAs and the OSPFv3 installer asserts Mode==transport on every SA, so both modes are proven; single-polarity annotated

| Polarity | Test | Kind and tier | Test status |
|---|---|---|---|
| positive | [`TestChildSAInstallsInDataplane`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go#L177) | unit/verify | not verified |
| positive | [`TestIPsecSAPerDestinationWithOSPFSelector`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install_test.go#L246) | unit/verify | revert, verified |

### [`RFC4301-4.2-1`](#rfc4301-4.2-1)

Note: A compliant implementation MUST NOT allow instantiation of an ESP SA that employs both NULL encryption and no integrity algorithm. (§4.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-11-19 after xfrmStateFromParams gained the XFRMA_SA_DIR mapping (xfrmSADirFromParams) and the outbound anti-replay refusal (3977cef525). RFC 4301 Section 4.2: "Note: A compliant implementation MUST NOT allow instantiation of an ESP SA that employs both NULL encryption and no integrity algorithm." Neither change touches the transform plan: planStateAlgos and xfrmAuthName still require an Auth transform on every non-AEAD ESP state, and an absent or none integrity algorithm is still refused before a state is returned. boundarySA now carries no replay window (outbound), so the ESP-null fixtures still build. vpp.go changed only in comments outside vppCryptoAlg and vppIntegAlg. The tagged units are unchanged: TestRFC4301ESPNullEncryptionWithIntegrityIsInstantiated asserts the null cipher with HMAC-SHA-256; TestRFC4301ESPNullEncryptionWithoutIntegrityIsRefused requires an error and a nil state for absent and none integrity; the VPP negative requires ErrNotSupported and no SAD message beside a valid-SA control; the OSPF pair refuses absent integrity and accepts SHA-256. Verdict stands.

|---|---|---|---|
| negative | [`TestRFC4301ESPNullEncryptionWithoutIntegrityIsRefused`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_esp_null_linux_test.go#L36) | unit/verify | revert, verified |
| negative | [`TestRFC4301VPPESPNullEncryptionWithoutIntegrityIsRefused`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_esp_null_vpp_test.go#L29) | unit/verify | mutant, verified |

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2-2`](#rfc4301-4.4.2-2)

the entry for an inbound SA in the SAD MUST be initially populated with the value or values negotiated at the time the SA was created. (§4.4.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-26 against HEAD c8584247bb (independent judge, clean clone, after a8797fd0c2, c8d3e41036, 1502b3034b). Section 4.4.2: "For each of the selectors defined in Section 4.4.1.1, the entry for an inbound SA in the SAD MUST be initially populated with the value or values negotiated at the time the SA was created." narrowChildSelectors changed in two places: substituteVirtualIP now drops a proposed TSi that does not contain the lease instead of replacing it (refusing with TS_UNACCEPTABLE when none is left), and the transport single-address filter is gated on transportSelectorsInPlay, which is false for a pool-bound peer whose transport request decideResponderTransportMode declined. Neither changes what is recorded: sa.NegotiatedPairs is still exactly the answered set (closedSubset of narrowed), and childPolicies/inboundStateSelector (unchanged) build the SAD selector from it, so SAD = negotiated holds on every path including the leased one. recordInitiatorSelectors gained answerNamesOnlyLeases before the ceiling, which only refuses; an accepted answer is still recorded as answered. Tagged carriers (TestRFC4301TransportStateSelectorIsTheNegotiatedPair, TestRFC4301MixedProtocolPairInstallsTheIntersection, TestRFC4301AuthResponseWithoutTSIsRefused, TestRFC4301InitiatorInstallsAnAddressRangeExactly, TestRFC4301InitiatorRefusesAnAnswerItCannotInstallExactly, TestRFC4301TransportInitiatorRefusesSeveralPairs, TestRFC4301InitiatorInstallsEveryAnsweredCombination) PASS at HEAD (./le job run, scoped -run). ./le rfc discriminate id RFC4301-4.4.2-2: ten mutant records, none stale, none unproven. Verdict stands.

### [`RFC4301-5.2-1`](#rfc4301-5.2-1)

Since the SPD-I is just a part of the SPD, if a packet that is looked up in the SPD-I cannot be matched to an entry there, then the packet MUST be discarded. (§4.4.1)

Audit verdict: unimplemented (no code path enforces the requirement), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go::applyConfig, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go::installUnmatched moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after 51799ad534 (owner ruling 5c) and the annotation correction that follows it. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1: "Since the SPD-I is just a part of the SPD, if a packet that is looked up in the SPD-I cannot be matched to an entry there, then the packet MUST be discarded." Producers read at HEAD: applyConfig now calls assertUnmatched, which on a reload runs installUnmatched and refuses the apply on any refused catch-all entry, and at start runs installUnmatchedAtStart, which keeps every accepted entry, refuses only a missing dataplane, logs the rest at Error under doctor-ipsec-spd-catch-all-missing and records it for the ipsec-spd-install check; placeXFRMInterfaceEntries and recordInterfaceCatchAllAtStart do the same for a bound XFRM interface's final entry, whose if_id 0 exemptions stay out. So a start whose catch-all was refused leaves a direction where an unmatched inbound packet meets no SPD-I entry and is delivered, a third path beside VPP (CatchAllSupported answers ErrNoNodeWideSPD) and the OSPF RFC 4552 manual-key entries. The corrected annotation names all three and the tests that show each (TestUnmatchedInstallFailureAtStartIsReportedByDoctor, TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureAtStartIsReportedByDoctor, TestUnmatchedInstallFailureFailsTheReload, TestUnmatchedWithNoDataplaneRefusesTheStart); every symbol it cites exists at HEAD. The gap stands.

No test carries RFC4301-5.2-1, so there is no linked test.

### [`RFC4301-5.2-2`](#rfc4301-5.2-2)

Then match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry to verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received. (§5.2)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry"; gap "verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received"; internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane now constructs both complete directional scopes before publishing either state, through internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope and dataplane.NewSAStateScope. Native positive and negative records prove single-pair whole and outer-fragmented bare ESP on an ESP-in-UDP state under the production SPD, including the state's selector rejection and a separate explicit SPD DISCARD. Those records do not establish general multi-pair or overlapping per-SA isolation. checkXFRMStateScopeCapabilities admits a state scope only on a receipt carrying feature bits 0 to 3 and 5 to 7, the ICMP reverse check, the forwarded ICMP error mapping and the forwarded ICMP error past a transit gate (required features 0xef); the carried K3 fragment guard is enrolled, a kernel whose receipt lacks bit 7, the runtime kernels qemu-1562c4ae and docker-1562c4ae included, is refused at Child SA install, and the bit-7 kernel docker-53c57c37 booted on arm64 on 2026-11-20 and ran the forwarded ICMP and fragment packet tests. Bounded scope, fragment and port-policy packet runs have passed, including a private whole-packet sibling-port cut that triggered the selected test's four own assertions. These observations do not complete the general isolation proof or renew its RFC records: the constrained permitted fragment and context cases now pass on the normal Ze kernel (eight selected packet/helper tests, including all 16 context rows), while separate broader RFC kernel-discrimination debt remains, with no private fault authorization or new C-enforcement credit. General isolation remains owned by plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md (AC-5, AC-7); ipsec-dual-form-receive-spd-bypass AC-4 now accepts the required normal Ze-kernel positive, negative and control packet tests for product closure. That acceptance does not remove this partial annotation or grant whole-requirement proof; the remaining RFC discrimination debt stays separate under the existing evidence rules; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_state_scope_linux.go::checkXFRMStateScopeCapabilities, internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the annotation's home moved from plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md to plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md (AC-5, AC-7), whose AC-7 homes this row by name; no tagged unit changed. RFC 4301 Section 5.2: "Then match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry to verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received." Tested: "match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry". Gap: "verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received". Producer: internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane, with internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope and internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_state_scope_linux.go::checkXFRMStateScopeCapabilities. The tunnel and transport SAD-selector tests deliver an in-selector packet and drop source, port and protocol misses at the state selector (XfrmInStateMismatch +1); the boundary units pin direction, exact port and template and refuse an out-of-mask port; the transport state selector is the negotiated pair; bare ESP whole and fragmented is policy-checked on an ESP-in-UDP state. All fixtures are single-pair, so per-SA isolation within a multi-pair or sibling set stays unproven (AC-7). Scoped evidence only; zero whole-requirement credit.

### [`RFC4301-4.1-6`](#rfc4301-4.1-6)

If an IPsec implementation supports multicast, then it MUST support multicast SAs using the algorithm below for mapping inbound IPsec datagrams to SAs. (§4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Judged on the path where Ze has multicast SAs (R24): the RFC 4552 manual OSPFv3 installer (buildIPsecInterfaceSAs/buildIPsecSA) run through onInterfaceUp over the real XFRM backend in a rootless user+net namespace; the lookup is netlink XFRM_MSG_GETSA against the kernel SAD (xfrm_state_lookup, the function XFRM input uses), not a mock (run by the judge 2026-09-30: child ran, not skipped). Positive: ff02::5 and ff02::6 share one SPI and each (group, SPI) lookup returns that group's own SA, i.e. step 2 (SPI + destination) of the section 4.1 search resolves an Any-Source Multicast group SA. Negative: after a control lookup, ff02::5 under an unconfigured SPI and the configured SPI to ff02::1 map to no SA (step 3 fails, packet has no SA). Step 1 (SPI+dst+src) is vacuous: Ze builds no source-specific multicast SA. Caveat recorded, not a finding against the tests: the ff02::1 case rests on Linux identifying the fe80:: unicast SA by (dst, SPI, proto) rather than the section 4.1 SPI(+protocol) identifier, so a literal step 3 would match that unicast SA; the stray-SPI case alone carries the negative without that reading. Records: revert buildIPsecSA, both polarities observed red.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1-3`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-3)

Every SPD SHOULD have a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it. (§4.4.1)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it"; gap "Every SPD"; the final DISCARD entry is installed and tested only in the XFRM SPDs the IKE engine owns: the node-wide SPD at if_id 0 (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go) and the SPD of each XFRM interface a peer binds with vti bind (interfaceSPDCatchAll, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go). Two kinds of SPD carry no final entry. The VPP SPDs: internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go::vppPolicyInterface refuses a policy bound to no interface and vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, so installUnmatched installs nothing on VPP and no SPD that vppBackend.ensureSPD creates and binds receives a final entry. The kernel SPD holding the OSPFv3 RFC 4552 manual-key entries: internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go::defaultDataplaneSource hands the OSPF installer whichever backend is loaded, and buildIPsecPolicies installs per-interface protocol 89 transport entries with no final entry beneath them, in force with no vpn ipsec block and after the IKE engine releases its SPD. Owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go::applyConfig, internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go::releaseSPD, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go::installUnmatched moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after 51799ad534 changed applyConfig, installUnmatched and installXFRMInterfaceEntries; no tagged unit changed. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1: "Every SPD SHOULD have a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it." Tested: "a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it". Gap: "Every SPD". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go::vppPolicyInterface. Producer change read: under owner ruling 5c a catch-all the dataplane refuses at the daemon's start no longer refuses the IPsec block (assertUnmatched, installUnmatchedAtStart; recordInterfaceCatchAllAtStart for an interface SPD): the start keeps every entry the dataplane accepted, logs at Error under doctor-ipsec-spd-catch-all-missing and reports it through ipsec-spd-install, while a reload still refuses. The successful install the tagged units observe is unchanged: the catch-all still goes first on the first apply and is replayed beneath every Ze write, an unmatched clear packet is discarded in the kernel at if_id 0 and on a bound XFRM interface, and a refused interface catch-all fails the reload. The annotation still holds: the final entry exists only in the XFRM SPDs the engine owns, none on VPP SPDs or beneath the OSPF protocol 89 entries. A start-time install refusal is a further window without the final entry, reported rather than prevented, which RFC4301-5.2-1 and 5.2-9 must name. Scoped evidence only; zero whole-requirement credit.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1-4`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-4)

Thus, a user or administrator MUST be able to order the entries to express a desired access control policy. (§4.4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_spd_order_total_test.go::TestSPDPolicyEqualOrderOnOverlapRefused moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after 06bd727362 changed the negative TestSPDPolicyEqualOrderOnOverlapRefused. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1: "Thus, a user or administrator MUST be able to order the entries to express a desired access control policy." The change is mechanical: orderTotalPeer lost its priority parameter and fixes policy-priority 2000 inside the helper, and every call passed 2000 before, so each case has the same peers, selectors and VTI binding, and no assertion changed. The test still drives verifySPDOrder: overlapping operator entries at one order, and an entry at an overlapping peer's policy-priority, are refused with the operator error naming both entries, the shared order and the exact fix; overlapping entries at distinct orders, disjoint entries at one order and an entry beside a peer bound to an XFRM interface are accepted. The positive TestSpdOperatorOrdersOverlappingPeers (both directions installed at ranks 500 and 1500, in that order) and the negative TestSpdUnstatedOrderTakesTheDefaultRank are unchanged. The negative's discrimination record was re-taken in 06bd727362. Verdict stands.

### [`RFC4301-7.4-2`](#rfc4301-7.4-2)

All implementations MUST support stateful fragment checking to accommodate BYPASS traffic for which a non-trivial port range is specified (§7.4; Appendix D.4 restates it as "implementations MUST support fragment reassembly for BYPASS/DISCARD traffic when port fields are specified")

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_fragment_integration_linux_test.go::TestXFRMPortScopedBypassIsNeverStoredOnTheForwardPath moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after adb172d967 tagged TestRFC4301CleartextFragmentsRespectOperatorDisposition (rfc4301_fragment_boundary_integration_linux_test.go after 0b920456f2), the local-delivery gap the previous weak verdict named. RFC 4301 Section 7.4: "All implementations MUST support stateful fragment checking to accommodate BYPASS traffic for which a non-trivial port range is specified." On Ze's kernel under unmatched discard, with IN BYPASS and DISCARD entries Ze installs on one UDP local port each, a whole and a fragmented cleartext datagram to the bypassed port are delivered, while whole and fragmented datagrams to the discarded port (XfrmInPolBlock) or the Child SA's port (template mismatch) are not; its guest record goes red with spdPolicyParams reverted. The test's reason for sending no lone tail holds: local input reassembles before any policy check, so a lone tail is never delivered whatever the SPD holds. Together with the IPv4 and IPv6 forward tests on Ze's kernel (whole and fragmented bypass datagrams cross, a lone tail and an unnamed port do not, red with spdForwardGathers reverted), the projection units on spdEntryHasTransit, the IN and OUT storage unit and the any-port negative, every direction Ze installs a port-scoped bypass on is observed at packet level. Residual, judged not to void the verdict: IPv6 local delivery is not observed and rests on Linux's INET6_PROTO_NOPOLICY Fragment header and the same family-agnostic installer the IPv4 test drives.

### [`RFC4301-4.1-12`](#rfc4301-4.1-12)

The indication of whether source and destination address matching is required to map inbound IPsec traffic to SAs MUST be set either as a side effect of manual SA configuration or via negotiation using an SA management protocol, e.g., IKE or Group Domain of Interpretation (GDOI) [RFC3547]. (§4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged pass 6. The unit now asserts what Linux actually reads for inbound source matching on a transport-mode SA: the state selector x->sel (xfrm_selector_match in __xfrm_policy_check), read back from the kernel for the ff02::5 and ff02::6 states found by (group, SPI), with source ::/0; a control AH state installed with selector source fe80::2/128 is read back first as a /128, so the read-back discriminates a pinned source from the wildcard. The props-source :: assertion is kept and correctly described as the outbound address check, not claimed. Doc comment, PREVENTS line and failure text now name the right mechanism. Producer read: buildIPsecSA sets Sel.Src to ospfWildcardNet(). Each probe runs in its own user+net namespace (no cross-test state). Revert record of buildIPsecSA observed red; author's overlay pinning Sel.Src also went red. single-polarity holds: multicast SAs exist only as RFC 4552 manual config, which always sets the indication, so no refusal path exists. Full ospf package -race green.

### [`RFC4301-4.1-9`](#rfc4301-4.1-9)

Audit verdict: weak (the tests pass over code that does not enforce the requirement), fresh. Re-judged 2026-11-19 after 3977cef525 (AC-21). RFC 4301 Section 4.1: "The receiver MUST process the packets from the different SAs without prejudice.  These requirements apply to both transport and tunnel mode SAs." The stale unit TestRFC4301BoundarySADEntryCarriesWhatTheKernelLooksUp now builds its two SAs with boundaryInboundSA (Dir SADirIn, ReplayWin 64) because xfrmStateFromParams refuses a window on an outbound state; its assertions (two distinct SAD entries, each its own SPI, same selector) are unchanged. TestRFC4301ParallelSAsWithOneSelectorAreEachReceived and TestRFC4301ParallelSAsAreHeldToTheOneSharedPolicy are unchanged since the last judgement: two tunnel Child SAs with one selector, first/second/first all delivered; an outside-source packet on each SA is refused at the SA selector (XfrmInStateMismatch +1 per SA, both SPD counters unchanged). parallelChildPair still never builds two transport-mode SAs, so the transport half of the sentence is not exercised. Verdict stays weak; a transport counterpart is owed.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1-6`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-6)

SPD-S: For traffic that is to be protected using IPsec, the entry consists of the values of the selectors that apply to the traffic to be protected via AH or ESP, controls on how to create SAs based on these selectors, and the parameters needed to effect this protection (e.g., algorithms, modes, etc.). (§4.4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-30 by an independent auditor after c41ebbaf93 moved the esp-group existence check from the deleted ValidateGroupRefs to the YANG leafref the commit path runs. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1 re-read in rfc/full/rfc4301.txt: "SPD-S: For traffic that is to be protected using IPsec, the entry consists of the values of the selectors that apply to the traffic to be protected via AH or ESP, controls on how to create SAs based on these selectors, and the parameters needed to effect this protection (e.g., algorithms, modes, etc.)." Q1 both units assert the three parts of the entry, which is what the RFC says. Q2 TestRFC4301SPDSEntryCarriesSelectorsSAControlsAndProtection parses one site-to-site peer through ParseIPsecConfig, requires config.ValidateCustomSections (referenceRefusals) to raise no esp-group/name refusal, and reads back the selector prefixes, the SA creation controls (connection-type, remote address, esp-group) and the protection parameters (mode transport, aes256/sha256), each compared exactly; TestRFC4301SPDSEntryRefusesAMissingOrUnusablePart breaks one part per case (undefined esp-group now refused by the leafref with 'no name "absent" exists at "../../../esp-group/name"', mode beet, unknown connection-type, a /33 selector) and requires a refusal naming that part. Revert records on config.go::parseSiteToSitePeer verify for both polarities. Q3 each negative case changes one leaf of an otherwise complete entry. Q4 all three parts of the entry are covered. The change replaced the producer of one refusal and kept every input and expected part, so the verdict stays enforced.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1-10`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-10)

If the SPD is not decorrelated, caching is not allowed and an ordered search of SPD MUST be performed to verify that inbound traffic arriving on an SA is consistent with the access control policy expressed in the SPD. (§4.4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-11-19. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1: "If the SPD is not decorrelated, caching is not allowed and an ordered search of SPD MUST be performed to verify that inbound traffic arriving on an SA is consistent with the access control policy expressed in the SPD." Since the state-selector binding the SA selector refuses an out-of-SA packet before the SPD is searched, so the tests were reworked. Positive TestRFC4301OrderedInboundSearchAdmitsOnTheFirstRankedEntry now narrows only the installed inbound PROTECT entry to 10.1.0.0/24 while the SA keeps 10.1.0.0/16, reads back the Child entry at PriorityChildSA allow over the catch-all, and proves three outcomes: a packet inside the PROTECT entry is delivered with XfrmInPolBlock unchanged; a packet inside the SA but outside the first-ranked PROTECT entry (10.1.1.5) is not delivered and moves XfrmInPolBlock by exactly one (the catch-all DISCARD decided after decryption); a packet outside the SA moves only XfrmInStateMismatch. That isolates the ordered SPD search on traffic the SA admitted, which is stronger than before. Negative TestRFC4301OrderedInboundSearchRefusesOnTheFirstRankedDiscard is unchanged (an operator inbound DISCARD ranked 1000 over the Child entry drops an in-selector packet on the SA, XfrmInPolBlock +1). The boundary SPD units are unchanged. Finding: TestRFC4301ParallelSAsAreHeldToTheOneSharedPolicy keeps an RFC4301-4.4.1-10 negative tag, but its body now asserts the refusal at the SA selector before any SPD lookup (XfrmInStateMismatch +1, XfrmInNoPols and XfrmInPolBlock unchanged); its claim says so honestly, yet it no longer evidences the SPD search this row requires. The verdict rests on the ordered pair; that tag should move to the SAD-selector rows (RFC4301-5.2-2, 5.2-11) or be removed, under ze-test-judge.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1-11`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-11)

SPD-I: For inbound traffic that is to be bypassed or discarded, the entry consists of the values of the selectors that apply to the traffic to be bypassed or discarded. (§4.4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-11 by an independent judge after the fix round (2691e815b1) added the disposition pair. RFC 4301 4.4.1, under "The SPD MUST permit a user or administrator to specify policy entries as follows:": "SPD-I: For inbound traffic that is to be bypassed or discarded, the entry consists of the values of the selectors that apply to the traffic to be bypassed or discarded." The engine units judged before (TestSPDPolicyMirrorsTheInboundSelector, TestRFC4301BypassReturnTrafficHasAnSPDIEntry, TestRFC4301BypassSPDIEntryIsNeverAnUnmirroredCopy) are unchanged and carry the selector half: the inbound entry holds the mirrored selectors of the traffic, never an unmirrored copy (records on spdPolicyParams). The added positive TestRFC4301SPDPermitsEachDispositionInEachDatabase carries the 'administrator can specify' half: bypass and discard with direction in and both parse through ParseIPsecConfig to the exact SPActionBypass/SPActionDiscard and SPDDirIn/SPDDirBoth; its claim names disposition and direction only, which is what it asserts. The added negative TestRFC4301SPDRefusesADispositionItDoesNotCarry refuses an absent action (not read as PROTECT, the SPAction zero value), protect, an unknown action and an unknown direction, each by its exact message. Revert records on ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseSPDPolicy for both.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1.1-2`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.1-2)

Note that the Local and Remote ports may not be available in the case of receipt of a fragmented packet or if the port fields have been protected by IPsec (encrypted); thus, a value of OPAQUE also MUST be supported. (§4.4.1.1)

Audit verdict: unimplemented (no code path enforces the requirement), fresh. First verdict 2026-11-20 by an independent agent. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1.1: "Note that the Local and Remote ports may not be available in the case of receipt of a fragmented packet or if the port fields have been protected by IPsec (encrypted); thus, a value of OPAQUE also MUST be supported." No tag, and none is owed until the code exists: the {gap} is true at the producers. Ze parses OPAQUE (traffic_selector.go PortSelectorFromWire reads the 65535/0 pair, parsePortSelector accepts `opaque` on a peer traffic selector) but programs it nowhere: PortSelector.ProgrammableForProtocol answers false for PortOpaque, checkPortProgrammable refuses it at commit with an operator error, and parseSPDPort refuses it in an operator SPD entry. So an operator cannot write an OPAQUE selector that takes effect, which is the missing behavior, owed by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-opaque-selector-port-mask.md. The annotation's ICMP part is also accurate at the producers: ICMPSelector.MatchesFragmentTypeCode, SPDPolicy.validateICMPFragments refusing an out or both entry holding type 0 code 0, and spdEntryHasTransit projecting no forward entry for one, covered only by untagged unit tests, with the packet proof owned by AC-9 of plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md. Scope finding for that AC: this row's sentence names only the Local and Remote ports; the ICMP type and code bullet of Section 4.4.1.1 says only "Note that the ICMP message type and code may not be available in the case of receipt of a fragmented packet", with no keyword. AC-9's packet tests can therefore prove at most an ICMP case of this row, carried as {partial}: they cannot lift the OPAQUE port gap, and a tag that cites them for the whole row would over-claim.

No test carries RFC4301-4.4.1.1-2, so there is no linked test.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1.1-3`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.1-3)

If the SA requires a port value other than ANY or OPAQUE, an arriving fragment without ports MUST be discarded (§4.4.1.1)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "If the SA requires a port value other than ANY or OPAQUE"; gap "an arriving fragment without ports MUST be discarded"; the tags prove that the entry Ze hands the kernel carries the exact port and that a port with a bit outside its mask is refused rather than widened (internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams). The discard of a fragment arriving on the SA is the kernel's state-scope check over the scope internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope installs with non-initial-fragment permission false (gokrazy/kernel/patches/0003-xfrm-state-scope.patch); no tagged test sends a fragment without ports on such an SA, nor asserts that installed permission, and the sibling and multi-pair probes (TestRFC4301SiblingSAsKeepInboundSelectorsBound, TestRFC4301MultipairSAsRejectSiblingScope) are untagged and unobserved; plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the annotation's home moved from plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md to plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md, whose AC-2 names this row; no tagged unit changed. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1.1: "If the SA requires a port value other than ANY or OPAQUE, an arriving fragment without ports MUST be discarded". Tested: "If the SA requires a port value other than ANY or OPAQUE". Gap: "an arriving fragment without ports MUST be discarded". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams, with internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope for the gap. TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryCarriesSelectorDirectionOrderAndTemplate keeps the exact inbound port 443 and TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryIsRefusedRatherThanWidened refuses a port with a bit outside its mask with no entry built, both with mutant records on xfrmSelectorPort: the required port reaches the kernel unwidened, the clause's precondition. childStateScope still installs non-initial-fragment permission false; no tagged test sends a fragment without ports on an exact-port SA under that permission, so the discard is unobserved, as AC-2 of the new home requires it be. Scoped evidence only; zero whole-requirement credit.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1.2-1`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.2-1)

One to N selector sets that correspond to the "condition" for applying a particular IPsec action. (§4.4.1.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent source rejudgment of RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1.2, including the full SPD-entry description: "One to N selector sets that correspond to the "condition" for applying a particular IPsec action." parseSPDPolicy constructs one set of local/remote prefixes, protocol and ports. N=1 meets this structural item; this does not establish multi-selector negotiation or the separately allocated users-MUST-NOT rule. Both tagged tests were read with holdsSelector: HoldsOneSelectorSet parses an operator tree and asserts exact local/remote prefixes, protocol 17, local port 53 and remote ANY; CannotCarryASecondSelectorSet counts four scalar prefix/port fields and rejects selector-bearing collections. Its reflection guard does not exhaust hypothetical recursively nested future models. The single-positive annotation is justified by this representation's fixed one-set cardinality, not by absence of all configuration error paths. The current exhaustive-ignore directives only document deliberately narrow reflection switches; assertions and selector construction remain substantive. Verdict enforced. No tests or discrimination replay executed by this auditor.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-5`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-5)

Sequence Counter Overflow: a flag indicating whether overflow of the sequence number counter should generate an auditable event and prevent transmission of additional packets on the SA, or whether rollover is permitted. (§4.4.2.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Single-polarity positive accepted: no config leaf or negotiation offers rollover or ESN. The unit builds ESP and AH SAs in both directions through xfrmStateFromParams and asserts OSeqMayWrap and ESN unset, so the kernel (xfrm_replay_overflow) audits the overflow and stops transmission. Would go red if the producer set rollover. Record: revert xfrmStateFromParams, observed red. Judge finding (not fixed): the stop-not-rollover decision is the zero value of netlink.XfrmState.OSeqMayWrap and xfrmStateFromParams never names it; under the principles rule a zero another component relies on is a guard that must be named (explicit assignment with the RFC quote). The test pins it, so it cannot vanish silently, but the producer still owes the named guard.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-6`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-6)

Anti-Replay Window: a 64-bit counter and a bit-map (or equivalent) used to determine whether an inbound AH or ESP packet is a replay. (§4.4.2.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-11-19 after 3977cef525 (AC-21, judge-approved test changes). RFC 4301 Section 4.4.2.1: "Anti-Replay Window: a 64-bit counter and a bit-map (or equivalent) used to determine whether an inbound AH or ESP packet is a replay." Both tagged units now build the state with boundaryInboundSA (Dir SADirIn), because the window belongs to the receiving state and xfrmStateFromParams now refuses a nonzero window on an outbound state, as Linux verify_newsa_info does. Assertions are unchanged: TestRFC4301BoundarySADEntryCarriesWhatTheKernelLooksUp requires ReplayWindow 64 on the built entry; TestRFC4301SADItemAntiReplayWindowIsInstalled requires windows 32 and 64 as given. installChildDataplane sets ReplayWin on the inbound state alone. Builder-level; the kernel performs the replay check on the window Ze installs. Single polarity as annotated, but the annotation's "so no input is refused" no longer holds literally: a window on an outbound or forward-direction state is refused (TestXFRMStateReplayWindowOnlyOnTheReceivingSA, untagged), which is not an inbound replay case. Verdict stands.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-11`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-11)

Lifetime of this SA: a time interval after which an SA must be replaced with a new SA (and new SPI) or terminated, plus an indication of which of these actions should occur. (§4.4.2.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent source rejudgment after the comment-only errcheck annotation on the hard-expiry timeout join. RFC 4301 section 4.4.2.1 says: Lifetime of this SA: a time interval after which an SA must be replaced with a new SA (and new SPI) or terminated, plus an indication of which of these actions should occur. All four tagged units were re-read against newLifetimeState, softExpired, hardExpired and maintainSA, including its rekey trigger and cleanup. The interval pair pins configured duration, replace-before-terminate, neither action before expiry and termination at the hard boundary. The owner-loop pair isolates soft and hard expiry: soft initiates CREATE_CHILD_SA with a new nonzero inbound SPI for the old Child SA and no timeout; hard returns errTimeout, leaves no pending rekey or Child SA, removes the old inbound SPI and sends no request. Removing termination or swapping the actions violates these exact assertions. The edited timeout path still fails unconditionally; no input, assertion or RFC claim changed. Prior proof provenance retained: the existing discrimination record carries revert observations for maintainSA and newLifetimeState; the previous audit additionally reported the author's soft/hard-swap overlay turning both action tests red. Those are historical observations, not executions by this judge. Discrimination records are unchanged. No runtime rerun, build, lint or RFC check was performed for this source-only rejudgment.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-14`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-14)

Bypass DF bit (T/F) -- applicable to tunnel mode SAs where both inner and outer headers are IPv4. (§4.4.2.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. First verdict 2026-11-20 by an independent agent. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.2.1: "Bypass DF bit (T/F) -- applicable to tunnel mode SAs where both inner and outer headers are IPv4." The row is a SAD field, so the boundary Ze owns is the XFRM state it builds. TestXFRMStateCarriesTheDFTreatment builds an outbound IPv4 tunnel-mode SA for copy and for clear and asserts NoPMTUDisc false and true exactly (xfrmStateFromParams); Linux xfrm4 tunnel encap writes the outer DF from the inner bit only when XFRM_STATE_NOPMTUDISC is clear, so the state holds the T/F choice. TestXFRMStateRefusesDFItCannotExpress takes the cases the field does not apply to (transport mode, IPv6 outer, and inbound) and asserts ErrSAOptionRefused, not ErrNotSupported, and a nil state. The pair is two distinct assertions on opposite sides of the clause's applicability. Unit-level only: the wire behaviour is proven by TestXFRMOuterHeaderCarriesTheDFTreatment under RFC4301-8.1-1/-2, not tagged here. Question 2 rests on the discrimination records the main thread is taking for these tags.

| Polarity | Test | Kind and tier | Test status |
|---|---|---|---|
| negative | [`TestXFRMStateRefusesDFItCannotExpress`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_df_linux_test.go#L67) | unit/verify | revert, verified |
| positive | [`TestXFRMStateCarriesTheDFTreatment`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_df_linux_test.go#L43) | unit/verify | revert, verified |

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-16`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-16)

Bypass DSCP (T/F) or map to unprotected DSCP values (array) if needed to restrict bypass of DSCP values -- applicable to tunnel mode SAs. (§4.4.2.1)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "Bypass DSCP (T/F)"; gap "map to unprotected DSCP values (array)"; the dscp-map leaf of a site-to-site peer sets the tunnel-mode SA's bypass-DSCP choice (copy is bypass true, a fixed value is bypass false and maps every inner DSCP to that one value), and the tags prove copy and a mapping to 0 reach the XFRM state and that the choice is refused on a transport-mode SA; no SAD entry holds a per-value map from inner to outer DSCP values, and on Linux XFRM internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDSCPFlag refuses a fixed value other than 0 (LL-XFRM-OUTER-DF-SET-DSCP, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md); plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. First verdict 2026-11-20 by an independent agent. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.2.1: "Bypass DSCP (T/F) or map to unprotected DSCP values (array) if needed to restrict bypass of DSCP values -- applicable to tunnel mode SAs." Tested: "Bypass DSCP (T/F)". Gap: "map to unprotected DSCP values (array)". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDSCPFlag. TestXFRMStateCarriesTheOuterDSCP asserts copy builds the outbound tunnel state without DontEncapDSCP and a mapping to 0 builds it with the flag (Linux then writes outer DSCP 0), so the state holds the bypass choice exactly. TestXFRMStateRefusesOuterDSCPItCannotWrite asserts a mapping on a transport-mode SA, outside the field's tunnel-mode applicability, is refused with ErrSAOptionRefused (its 46 and inbound cases serve other rows). The scoped assertions hold. The per-value array is honestly disclosed as unbuilt, and a fixed value other than 0 is refused on Linux XFRM by xfrmDSCPFlag (LL-XFRM-OUTER-DF-SET-DSCP), homed in plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md, which lists this row. No whole-requirement credit.

### [`RFC4301-5.1-4`](#rfc4301-5.1-4)

Match the packet headers against the cache for the SPD specified by the SPD-ID from step 1. (§5.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_outbound_steps_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301OutboundPacketTakesTheDispositionOfTheEntryItMatches moved. Re-judged 2026-11-10 (independent judge, authored none of spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps). The stale unit TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryIsRefusedRatherThanWidened now refuses a port with a bit outside its mask (443 and 0x0100, each under 0x00ff) in both directions, where it refused a 0x00ff mask outright: xfrmSelectorPort now holds a mask-aligned partial mask exactly (it is how an ICMP code block is installed) and refuses only port bits the kernel would ignore. The RFC4301-5.1-4 negative claim says exactly that refusal for the outbound entry. Every earlier refusal case stays, a second input is added, so the unit proves at least what it did. The stack proof stays the two engine outbound-steps units, unchanged. Verdict stands. Prior judgement: Step 2 (match the outbound packet headers against the SPD). Linux keeps no SPD cache, so the match is the kernel SPD lookup over the entries Ze installs through createFirstChildSA, installSPDPolicies and installUnmatched. Positive: from one source, a datagram to 10.1.0.5 meets the Child SA PROTECT entry (ESP at the tunnel endpoint under child.OutboundSPI, XfrmOutNoStates unchanged) and one to 10.3.0.5:7001 meets the operator BYPASS entry (delivered clear, no ESP). Negative: 10.3.0.5 on port 7009, differing from the BYPASS entry only by port, is not bypassed: EPERM, XfrmOutPolBlock +1, not delivered, so the match reads the headers, not the destination alone. Judge ran both units in their own user+net namespace: PASS, not skipped. Records: xfrmPolicyFromParams panic revert red both polarities; author's manual overlays (bypass->BLOCK reddens the positive, discard->ALLOW reddens the negative, scratch p7-ov-*.log) confirm each polarity reads the disposition. Re-judged 2026-09-30 (leftovers): the retired lead-in RFC4301-5.1-1's tags moved here onto the dataplane SPD boundary units, as supplementary builder-level cover of step 2's input: the positive asserts the outbound entry's out direction, 10.0.0.0/24 -> 10.1.0.0/24 prefixes and exact source port 443; the negative asserts a 0x00ff port mask is refused (nil policy) for SADirOut and SADirIn rather than widened. Claims match the assertions. Records: xfrmPolicyFromParams panic revert observed red both polarities. Judge ran both: PASS. Stack proof stays the two engine units.

### [`RFC4301-5.1-5`](#rfc4301-5.1-5)

If there is a match, then process the packet as specified by the matching cache entry, i.e., BYPASS, DISCARD, or PROTECT using AH or ESP. (§5.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_outbound_steps_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301OutboundPacketTakesTheDispositionOfTheEntryItMatches moved. Step 3a: process as the matching entry says, BYPASS, DISCARD or PROTECT. PROTECT: the datagram reaches the remote endpoint as ESP carrying the Child SA outbound SPI, no XfrmOutNoStates. BYPASS: delivered in the clear, and no ESP copy on the raw socket. DISCARD: operator DISCARD entry refuses the write with EPERM, XfrmOutPolBlock +1, not delivered. All three dispositions asserted on kernel counters and delivery; AH is not installed by IKE (ESP only), so the PROTECT case is ESP. Records: producer revert red both polarities; overlays discard->ALLOW and bypass->BLOCK each redden exactly the unit that asserts that disposition.

### [`RFC4301-5.1-6`](#rfc4301-5.1-6)

If no match is found in the cache, search the SPD (SPD-S and SPD-O parts) specified by SPD-ID. (§5.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_outbound_steps_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301OutboundPacketTakesTheDispositionOfTheEntryItMatches moved. Step 3b SPD search. With no kernel SPD cache every packet, including the first of a flow, is looked up in the SPD (SPD-S: the Child SA PROTECT entry; SPD-O: operator BYPASS/DISCARD and the catch-all). Positive: first packets on the PROTECT and BYPASS entries take those entries' dispositions. Negative: a packet selecting no operator entry takes the catch-all's discard (EPERM, XfrmOutPolBlock +1), not a neighbouring entry's bypass. Claims name the cache absence rather than proving a cache. Records as 5.1-4.

Re-judged 2026-11-19 after child.go (forward entries, direction, rekey source) and ipsec/spd_policy.go (rank range) changed. RFC 4301 Section 5.1: "If the SPD entry calls for PROTECT, i.e., creation of an SA, the key management mechanism (e.g., IKEv2) is invoked to create the SA." installChildSA still installs a Child SA's PROTECT entries only with the SA an IKE exchange already negotiated. parseSPDPolicy still accepts only bypass and discard as an operator action. ### [`RFC4301-5.1.2.1-1`](#rfc4301-5.1.2.1-1)

If the packet will immediately enter a domain for which the DSCP value in the outer header is not appropriate, that value MUST be mapped to an appropriate value for the domain (§5.1.2.1)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "that value MUST be mapped"; gap "to an appropriate value for the domain"; dscp-map copies the inner DSCP or fixes one outer DSCP per peer, and the tags prove that treatment reaches the SA on VPP for any value and on Linux XFRM for copy and 0, and that a value a dataplane cannot write is refused at commit and again at install rather than installed as another; Gap is an appropriate value Ze cannot write: a fixed value 1 to 63 on Linux XFRM, which internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDSCPFlag refuses because the XFRM state writes only the inner DSCP or 0 (LL-XFRM-OUTER-DF-SET-DSCP, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint and not a kernel limit: an nftables rewrite of the outer header could write it), an owner-decided refusal never counted conformant whose Ze kernel patch is planned after the first release (plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md), and a per-class mapping table, which is not built; plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. First verdict 2026-11-20 by an independent agent, after the annotation was re-homed to plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md (which lists this row); no tagged unit changed. RFC 4301 Section 5.1.2.1: "If the packet will immediately enter a domain for which the DSCP value in the outer header is not appropriate, that value MUST be mapped to an appropriate value for the domain". Tested: "that value MUST be mapped". Gap: "to an appropriate value for the domain". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDSCPFlag. Positives: the dscp-map leaf parses copy or 0 to 63 per peer (TestDSCPMapIsConfigurablePerPeer); the engine carries the peer's mapping onto the outbound SA with the inbound kept copy (TestOuterHeaderDSCPReachesTheBackend) and across rekey (TestRekeyKeepsTheOuterHeaderTreatment); XFRM builds DontEncapDSCP for 0 and not for copy (TestXFRMStateCarriesTheOuterDSCP); VPP sends ENCAP_COPY_DSCP or Dscp 46 (TestVPPInstallSACarriesTheOuterDSCP); and on the wire an EF inner datagram leaves with DSCP 46 under copy and 0 under the mapping (TestXFRMOuterHeaderCarriesTheOuterDSCP), which asserts one header per send. Negatives: a value XFRM cannot write is refused at state build, at config verify (TestXFRMSAOptionsSupportedRefusesOuterDSCPItCannotWrite, TestVerifyPeerSAOptionsRefusesWhatTheBackendCannotApply, naming the peer) and at install without a degraded Child SA (TestChildSAOptionRefusalIsNotKeptDegraded). The scoped claim holds: the mapping is applied, never silently replaced. The gap (a fixed 1 to 63 on Linux XFRM, a per-class table) stays unmet and is never counted conformant.

### [`RFC4301-5.2-9`](#rfc4301-5.2-9)

(No SAs are created in response to receipt of a packet that requires IPsec protection; only BYPASS or DISCARD cache entries can be created this way.) If there is no match, discard the traffic. (§5.2)

Audit verdict: unimplemented (no code path enforces the requirement), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go::applyConfig, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go::installUnmatched moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after 51799ad534 (owner ruling 5c) and the annotation correction that follows it. RFC 4301 Section 5.2: "(No SAs are created in response to receipt of a packet that requires IPsec protection; only BYPASS or DISCARD cache entries can be created this way.) If there is no match, discard the traffic." Same producers and paths as RFC4301-5.2-1: a reload still refuses a catch-all it cannot install, but the daemon's start installs what the dataplane accepts and reports the rest (assertUnmatched, installUnmatchedAtStart, recordInterfaceCatchAllAtStart), so after such a start an inbound packet no entry matches is delivered in the refused direction until a restart or a vpn ipsec commit installs the entry. VPP still holds no catch-all, and the OSPF manual-key entries still have no final DISCARD outside the engine's window. The corrected annotation states the three paths accurately and cites tests that exist at HEAD. The gap stands.

No test carries RFC4301-5.2-9, so there is no linked test.

### [`RFC4301-5.2-11`](#rfc4301-5.2-11)

If an IPsec system receives an inbound packet on an SA and the packet's header fields are not consistent with the selectors for the SA, it MUST discard the packet. (§5.2)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "it MUST discard the packet"; gap "If an IPsec system receives an inbound packet on an SA"; the split is by case, not by clause: Tested covers an SA negotiated for a single selector pair, whose inbound XFRM state carries that pair as its selector (internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane), so a tunnel-mode or transport-mode packet, or an ICMP Echo Request, whose header fields miss it is dropped at the state's selector check (XfrmInStateMismatch) and a packet inside it is delivered; Gap is a packet arriving on one SA of a multi-pair or sibling set whose header fields match a sibling's selectors and not this SA's, which no packet test observes, the same per-SA isolation case the RFC4301-5.2-2 partial leaves open; plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the annotation's home moved to plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md, whose AC-7 homes this row by name, and after TestRFC4301NonErrorICMPOutsideSelectorsIsDiscarded changed: its counter reader icmpErrSNMP(t, "InMsgs") became icmpInMsgs(t), the same Icmp InMsgs read with the same assertions (a parameter-only rename). RFC 4301 Section 5.2: "If an IPsec system receives an inbound packet on an SA and the packet's header fields are not consistent with the selectors for the SA, it MUST discard the packet." Tested: "it MUST discard the packet". Gap: "If an IPsec system receives an inbound packet on an SA". Producer: internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane. The tunnel-mode test delivers an in-selector packet first, then for source, port and protocol misses requires no delivery and XfrmInStateMismatch +1 with XfrmInNoPols and XfrmInPolBlock unchanged, isolating the SA's own selector; the transport-mode test drops a port-6000 packet and an ICMP echo at the state (XfrmInStateMismatch +1); the non-error test drops an Echo Request whose outer headers miss the state selector (XfrmInStateMismatch +1, InMsgs not counting it) beside a delivered consistent error. Discard and delivery are distinct assertions. Single-pair fixtures only: a packet on one SA of a multi-pair or sibling set matching a sibling's selectors is unobserved. Scoped evidence only; zero whole-requirement credit.

### [`RFC4301-6-1`](#rfc4301-6-1)

Disposition of non-error, ICMP messages (that are not addressed to the IPsec implementation itself) MUST be explicitly accounted for using SPD entries. (§6)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "MUST be explicitly accounted for using SPD entries"; gap "Disposition of non-error, ICMP messages"; the split is by case, not by clause: Tested covers a forwarded ICMPv4 Echo Request between a protected host and the outside, not addressed to Ze, which the catch-all discards while no operator entry names it, which an operator BYPASS for ICMP forwards in clear, and which an operator out DISCARD for ICMP drops at its OUT policy, the bypass decided by the forward decision and gates the operator entry's projection installs (internal/component/ike/engine/spd_policy.go::spdTransitPolicies); Gap is the transit ICMPv6 non-error message, to which the first paragraph of Section 6 applies the whole section, and which no packet test sends, because the namespace lab and packet harness of these tests is IPv4 only; plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/spd_policy.go::spdTransitPolicies moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the row gained a {partial} annotation, replacing this agent's earlier enforced verdict, which had left the ICMPv6 case as a residual. RFC 4301 Section 6: "Disposition of non-error, ICMP messages (that are not addressed to the IPsec implementation itself) MUST be explicitly accounted for using SPD entries." and "The discussion in this section applies to ICMPv6 as well as to ICMPv4." Tested: "MUST be explicitly accounted for using SPD entries"; Gap: "Disposition of non-error, ICMP messages", split by case: the transit ICMPv6 non-error message. Producer: internal/component/ike/engine/spd_policy.go::spdTransitPolicies, the first path::Symbol in the reason. It is the right producer for the bypass: it builds the FWD decision and the transit gates that carry a forwarded Echo, and the gate the negative's discard must out-rank. The tested scope is enforced. Positive, TestRFC4301OperatorBypassForwardsTransitEchoInClear: under `unmatched discard` with no Child SA, a transit Echo Request (not addressed to Ze) is first not forwarded in either direction while no operator entry names it; then a both-direction operator BYPASS for ICMP, accepted by verifySPDOrder, installed through installSPDPolicies and read back from the kernel (transitWantInstalled, which also requires a forward policy), forwards a second Echo in each direction, seen in clear on the egress link. The entry is the only change between the two observations. Negative, TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPErrorUnderOperatorDiscardMapsToReturnSA, Echo case: an operator out DISCARD for ICMP, with an in BYPASS whose OUT transit gate would otherwise carry the Echo in clear, gives XfrmOutPolBlock +1 and no copy at the peer, protected or in clear. The gap is honest: every case is IPv4, and AC-8 of plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md names RFC4301-6-1's ICMPv6 pair. Wording finding, not a scope defect: the reason says the first paragraph of Section 6 applies the section to ICMPv6, but that sentence is the second paragraph (the first ends "using SPD entries."); the 6.2-3, -4 and -5 reasons say the same. Record finding: every 6-1 record breaks spdTransitPolicies by a panic, which proves reachability only, and the discard test still holds a positive record for a polarity it no longer claims.

### [`RFC4301-6.2-3`](#rfc4301-6.2-3)

If no SA exists that would carry the outbound ICMP message in question, and if no SPD entry would allow carriage of this outbound ICMP error message, then an IPsec implementation MUST map the message to the SA that would carry the return traffic associated with the packet that triggered the ICMP error message. (§6.2)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "MUST map the message to the SA that would carry the return traffic"; gap "If no SA exists that would carry the outbound ICMP message in question"; the split is by case, not by clause: Tested covers an ICMPv4 error, forwarded from the protected side or generated by Ze for an authenticated trigger, that no SA and no SPD entry carries, mapped by the kernel to the return SA of a Child SA whose outbound protect policy carries XFRM_POLICY_ICMP, without which the kernel's ICMP route lookup on the reversed header finds no policy (internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams), and the commit-time refusal of a bypass the mapping would override for protocols 1 and 58 alike; Gap is the ICMPv6 error, to which the first paragraph of Section 6 applies the whole section, and which no packet test sends, because the Child SA and ICMP packet harness of these tests is IPv4 only; plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), shifted: internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_icmp_return_integration_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPErrorsMapUnderDiscard, internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_icmp_return_integration_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPErrorsMapUnderDiscard#2, internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_icmp_return_integration_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301LocalGeneratedICMPUsesReturnSA moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the row gained a {partial} annotation and its producer clause was corrected. RFC 4301 Section 6.2: "If no SA exists that would carry the outbound ICMP message in question, and if no SPD entry would allow carriage of this outbound ICMP error message, then an IPsec implementation MUST map the message to the SA that would carry the return traffic associated with the packet that triggered the ICMP error message." Tested: "MUST map the message to the SA that would carry the return traffic"; Gap: "If no SA exists that would carry the outbound ICMP message in question", split by case: the ICMPv6 error. Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams, the first path::Symbol in the reason and the right one: it sets XFRM_POLICY_ICMP on the outbound protect policy from SPParams.ICMPErrorReturn (set by engine/child.go childPairPolicy), the flag xfrm_lookup_with_ifid requires before an XFRM_LOOKUP_ICMP lookup on the reversed header (icmp_route_lookup, xfrm_out_fwd_icmp) may use the policy; the earlier producer, xfrmStateFromParams, set the inbound reverse-check flag of 6.2-4 and was wrong. The tested scope is enforced. TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPErrorsMapUnderDiscard: under `unmatched discard` with no operator entry, a forwarded Destination Unreachable that no SA carries (the Child's policies are UDP only) meets the OUT catch-all (XfrmOutPolBlock +1) and is decrypted at the outside peer from the Child SA's own SPI byte for byte, beside a UDP control through that SA, with no copy in clear; its negative, the same error quoting traffic no SA carries, is seen neither protected nor in clear ("If no such SA exists, no SA will be created"). TestRFC4301LocalGeneratedICMPUsesReturnSA covers an error Ze generates for an authenticated closed-port trigger (NoPorts +1), decrypted from the return SA; the decoder also asserts the installed return policy's ICMPErrorReturn flag, and its untagged sibling without the flag is the control. The condition is held by commit refusals (refuseICMPErrorBypass: TestICMPOverlapBypassCatchAll, TestICMPOverlapBypassEntryRefused, TestICMPOverlapBypassTransitGate) with accepted disjoint and unchecked controls (TestICMPOverlapBypassEntryAccepted), covering protocols 1 and 58. The gap is honest: Section 6 says "The discussion in this section applies to ICMPv6 as well as to ICMPv4", every packet case is ICMPv4, and AC-8 of plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md owns the ICMPv6 case. Record finding: the new packet-test records break their producers (appendChildOutboundForwardPolicy, spdCatchAllEntries, xfrmPolicyFromParams) by a panic, which proves reachability only; the overlap-gate records are real mutants.

### [`RFC4301-6.2-4`](#rfc4301-6.2-4)

If an IPsec implementation receives an inbound ICMP error message on an SA, and the IP and ICMP headers of the message do not match the traffic selectors for the SA, the receiver MUST process the received message in a special fashion. Specifically, the receiver must extract the header of the triggering packet from the ICMP payload, and reverse fields as described above to determine if the packet is consistent with the selectors for the SA via which the ICMP error message was received. (§6.2)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "the receiver MUST process the received message in a special fashion"; gap "If an IPsec implementation receives an inbound ICMP error message on an SA"; the split is by case, not by clause: Tested covers an ICMPv4 error received on a Child SA, delivered to this node or forwarded to the protected host, whose own headers miss the SA's selectors and whose quoted header, reversed, is checked against them by the kernel because the inbound state carries XFRM_STATE_ICMP (internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams); Gap is the inbound ICMPv6 error, to which the first paragraph of Section 6 applies the whole section, and which no packet test sends, because the Child SA and ICMP packet harness of these tests is IPv4 only; plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), shifted: internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_forward_icmp_integration_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPUsesPayloadPolicy moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the row gained a {partial} annotation. RFC 4301 Section 6.2: "If an IPsec implementation receives an inbound ICMP error message on an SA, and the IP and ICMP headers of the message do not match the traffic selectors for the SA, the receiver MUST process the received message in a special fashion. Specifically, the receiver must extract the header of the triggering packet from the ICMP payload, and reverse fields as described above to determine if the packet is consistent with the selectors for the SA via which the ICMP error message was received." Tested: "the receiver MUST process the received message in a special fashion"; Gap: "If an IPsec implementation receives an inbound ICMP error message on an SA", split by case: the ICMPv6 error. Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams, which sets XFRM_STATE_ICMP on the inbound state, the flag icmp_rcv and icmpv6_rcv read; it is the right producer for this row. The tested scope is enforced: TestICMPErrorPayloadHeaderCheckedOnLocalDelivery delivers an error addressed to Ze whose outer headers miss the SA's policy when its reversed quote matches, drops one whose quote matches nothing, and its no-flag control ties the delivery to the payload check; TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPUsesPayloadPolicy forwards, through a real Child SA after a UDP control crossed it, an error whose own headers miss the SA's UDP selectors and whose reversed quote is the SA's traffic, byte for byte to the protected host; TestRFC4301NonErrorICMPOutsideSelectorsIsDiscarded is the negative outside the clause (an Echo Request with the same bytes gets no special processing). The gap is honest: Section 6 says "The discussion in this section applies to ICMPv6 as well as to ICMPv4", every packet here is ICMPv4, and the ICMPv6 case is owed by AC-8 of plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md. Record finding: the record on the forwarded test breaks xfrmStateFromParams by a panic, which proves reachability only; the dataplane records are real mutants.

### [`RFC4301-6.2-5`](#rfc4301-6.2-5)

If the packet fails this check, the IPsec implementation MUST NOT forwarded the ICMP message to the destination. (§6.2)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "MUST NOT forwarded the ICMP message to the destination"; gap "If the packet fails this check"; the split is by case, not by clause: Tested covers an ICMPv4 error received on a Child SA whose quoted header, reversed, is traffic the SA does not carry, refused by the kernel's reverse check on the inbound state that carries XFRM_STATE_ICMP (internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams) and kept from this node and from the protected host; Gap is the ICMPv6 error failing that check, to which the first paragraph of Section 6 applies the whole section, and which no packet test sends, because the Child SA and ICMP packet harness of these tests is IPv4 only; plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), shifted: internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_forward_icmp_integration_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPUsesPayloadPolicy, internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_forward_icmp_integration_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPUsesPayloadPolicy#2 moved. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the row gained a {partial} annotation. RFC 4301 Section 6.2: "If the packet fails this check, the IPsec implementation MUST NOT forwarded the ICMP message to the destination." Tested: "MUST NOT forwarded the ICMP message to the destination"; Gap: "If the packet fails this check", split by case: the ICMPv6 error. Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams (XFRM_STATE_ICMP on the inbound state), the right producer. The tested scope is enforced, the violating input refused in both receiver cases: TestRFC4301ForwardedICMPUsesPayloadPolicy sends on a real Child SA an error whose reversed quote is traffic the SA does not carry, waits for the kernel's reject counter to rise by one, and asserts it never reaches the protected host while the UDP control after it does; the same error quoting the SA's traffic is forwarded, so the refusal is tied to the check. TestICMPErrorPayloadHeaderCheckedOnLocalDelivery gives the same pair for an error addressed to Ze, with a no-flag control. The gap is honest: Section 6 says "The discussion in this section applies to ICMPv6 as well as to ICMPv4", no ICMPv6 error is exercised, and AC-8 of plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md owns it. Label finding, not a test defect: the two tests name the polarities oppositely (the local-delivery test calls the refusal negative, the forwarded test calls it positive); each test carries both. Record finding: the two records on the forwarded test break xfrmStateFromParams by a panic, which proves reachability only, and without the flag the inconsistent error is refused anyway, so that break cannot show the refusal discriminates; the dataplane records are real mutants.

### [`RFC4301-7.1-2`](#rfc4301-7.1-2)

If the SA will carry traffic for specified protocols, the selector set for the SA MUST specify the port fields (or ICMP type/code or Mobility Header type) as ANY. (§7.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-19 (independent judge, after 4c41f84038, which replaced childPolicyParams with childPolicies: one inbound and one outbound policy per negotiated pair, all bound to the one SA, plus inboundStateSelector for the transport-mode state). RFC 4301 Section 7.1: "If the SA will carry traffic for specified protocols, the selector set for the SA MUST specify the port fields (or ICMP type/code or Mobility Header type) as ANY." Prior verdict enforced. selectorPort now takes the side (tsSelector) instead of (child, local); childPolicies picks local/remote by SelectorsLocalIsTSi exactly as the old side choice did, and the ANY/single/OPAQUE mapping is byte-identical. The units changed only to childPolicies(rfc4301ChildWith(pair), dir)[0]; rfc4301ChildWith carries one pair, so the slice holds one policy. Each negotiated pair now gets its own protocol and ports, so a multi-pair SA no longer inherits pair 0's. Positive asserts UpperProto 17 and both ports ANY in both directions; negative asserts no ExactPortMatch(8080/9090/0) leaks under ANY. PASS at HEAD. Verdict stands. Owed: both revert records (break on selectorPort) report unit-changed and must be re-observed.

| positive | [`TestRFC4301ProtocolScopedSASpecifiesPortsAsAny`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_portless_test.go#L99) | unit/verify | revert, verified |

### [`RFC4301-7.1-3`](#rfc4301-7.1-3)

If the SA will carry traffic without regard to a specific protocol value (i.e., ANY is specified as the (Next Layer) protocol selector value), then the port field values are undefined and MUST be set to ANY as well. (§7.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-19 (independent judge, after 4c41f84038, which replaced childPolicyParams with childPolicies: one inbound and one outbound policy per negotiated pair, all bound to the one SA, plus inboundStateSelector for the transport-mode state). RFC 4301 Section 7.1: "If the SA will carry traffic without regard to a specific protocol value (i.e., ANY is specified as the (Next Layer) protocol selector value), then the port field values are undefined and MUST be set to ANY as well." Prior verdict enforced. TestRFC4301ProtocolAnyChildSACarriesAnyPorts changed only to childPolicies(child, dir)[0] over a one-pair narrowing result; it still asserts UpperProto 0 and both ports ANY in and out. The negative (programmableSelector refuses protocol ANY with a port) and the ipsec supplementary units are unchanged. The new transport-state cover also widens ports to ANY whenever the protocol widens to any (inboundStateSelector), consistent with this sentence. PASS at HEAD. Verdict stands. Owed: the positive's revert record (break on selectorPort) reports unit-changed and must be re-observed.
