### [`RFC4301-7.3-4`](#rfc4301-7.3-4)

However, a receiver MUST discard non-initial fragments that arrive on an SA with non-trivial port (or ICMP type/code or MH type) selector values unless this feature has been negotiated. (§7.3)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "an SA with non-trivial port (or ICMP type/code or MH type) selector values"; gap "a receiver MUST discard non-initial fragments that arrive on"; the tags prove that the inbound entry keeps its exact port, that a widened port is refused (internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams), and that the responder narrows away a port 0 or ICMP type 0 code 0 selector every fragment matches (narrowSelectors). Ze never negotiates NON_FIRST_FRAGMENTS_ALSO, and internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope installs every Child SA's complete scope with non-initial-fragment permission false, which the kernel's state-scope check (gokrazy/kernel/patches/0003-xfrm-state-scope.patch) enforces; no tagged test sends a non-initial fragment on an exact-port SA under that production permission and observes the discard (the fragment packet tests use fragmentPermittedChild, which grants it), and the sibling and multi-pair probes TestRFC4301SiblingSAsKeepInboundSelectorsBound and TestRFC4301MultipairSAsRejectSiblingScope are untagged and unobserved; plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. Re-judged 2026-11-20 by an independent agent after the annotation's home moved to plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md, whose AC-2 names this row; no tagged unit changed. RFC 4301 Section 7.3: "However, a receiver MUST discard non-initial fragments that arrive on an SA with non-trivial port (or ICMP type/code or MH type) selector values unless this feature has been negotiated." Tested: "an SA with non-trivial port (or ICMP type/code or MH type) selector values". Gap: "a receiver MUST discard non-initial fragments that arrive on". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams, with internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope for the gap. The boundary positive keeps the exact inbound port 443, its negative refuses a port outside its mask, and TestNarrowingRefusesASelectorEveryFragmentMatches narrows away TCP port 0 and ICMP type 0 code 0 while accepting port 179, ICMP 0/3, ICMP 8/0 and any port unchanged. The MH type in the tested span has no case of its own. childStateScope still installs permission false and Ze sends no NON_FIRST_FRAGMENTS_ALSO; no tagged test observes the discard under that permission, and the sibling and multi-pair probes are untagged. Scoped evidence only; zero whole-requirement credit.

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "discard non-initial fragments that do not comply with the security policy applied to the overall packet"; gap "the receiver"; the packet tests prove it for a tunnel-mode Child SA delivering to this node: the fragments of a compliant inner packet are reassembled and delivered, and those of a packet whose source is outside the selector are discarded by the SA's own selector (XfrmInStateMismatch +1), on the state internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane installs. The gateway receiver is not proven: no tagged test sends the fragments of a non-compliant inner packet on an SA whose decapsulated packet Ze forwards, where the kernel checks FWD after K3's fragment guard gathers it (gokrazy/kernel/patches/0005-xfrm-fragment-guard.patch); plan/release/validation/spec-ipsec-fragment-receive-and-kernel-proof.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Tested: "discard non-initial fragments that do not comply with the security policy applied to the overall packet". Gap: "the receiver". Producer: internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane. TestRFC4301FragmentsOfACompliantPacketAreDelivered and TestRFC4301FragmentsOfANonCompliantPacketAreDiscarded send the non-initial fragment first, each in its own ESP packet on a tunnel-mode SA delivering to this node: the compliant packet is reassembled and delivered with XfrmInNoPols unchanged, the outside-source one is not delivered and moves XfrmInStateMismatch by exactly one. They grant fragment permission (fragmentPermittedChild), so the selector check makes the discard. The boundary units pin the inbound selector and template. The gateway receiver (FWD after K3 gathers) has no tagged test, as the annotation says. Scoped evidence only; zero whole-requirement credit.

### [`RFC4301-8.1-2`](#rfc4301-8.1-2)

This means that it MUST be possible to configure the implementation's treatment of the DF bit (set, clear, copy from inner header) for each SA. (§8.1)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "clear, copy from inner header"; gap "set"; copy and clear are configurable per peer (df-bit), and the tags prove each reaches the SA on Linux XFRM and VPP and the wire, and that a treatment a dataplane cannot apply is refused at commit and again at install rather than installed as another; Gap is DF set, which internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDFFlag and internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go::vppDFError refuse because neither the XFRM state nor VPP's ESP encrypt path writes a set DF bit on an ESP tunnel header (LL-XFRM-OUTER-DF-SET-DSCP, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint and not a kernel limit: an nftables rewrite of the outer header could write it); the owner decided that refusal is the behaviour for the first release, with a Ze kernel patch after it (plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md), and it is never counted conformant; plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. First verdict 2026-11-20 by an independent agent, after the annotation was re-homed to plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md (which lists this row); no tagged unit changed. RFC 4301 Section 8.1: "This means that it MUST be possible to configure the implementation's treatment of the DF bit (set, clear, copy from inner header) for each SA." Tested: "clear, copy from inner header". Gap: "set". Producer: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDFFlag and internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go::vppDFError. Positives: df-bit parses per peer with absent as copy (TestDFBitIsConfigurablePerPeer); the engine hands the outbound SA the peer's treatment and the inbound copy, exactly one of each (TestDFBitTreatmentReachesTheBackend), and rekey keeps it (TestRekeyKeepsTheOuterHeaderTreatment); XFRM sets NoPMTUDisc for clear and not for copy (TestXFRMStateCarriesTheDFTreatment); VPP sends ENCAP_COPY_DF for copy and neither DF flag for clear (TestVPPInstallSACarriesTheDFTreatment); on the wire clear emits a clear outer DF for a set inner bit and copy follows the inner bit (TestXFRMOuterHeaderCarriesTheDFTreatment). Negatives: set, and a treatment on a transport, inbound or IPv6-outer SA, are refused with ErrSAOptionRefused on XFRM and VPP with nothing installed or sent (TestXFRMStateRefusesDFItCannotExpress, TestVPPInstallSARefusesDFItCannotExpress), and ValidateOuterHeader refuses set and an inapplicable clear (TestDFBitValidation). The scoped claim holds; DF set is refused, never installed as another treatment, and stays unmet.

### [`RFC4301-8.2.1-1`](#rfc4301-8.2.1-1)

When such traffic arrives, if the traffic would exceed the updated PMTU value the traffic MUST be handled as follows: Case 1: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv4 and has the DF bit set. The implementation SHOULD discard the packet and send a PMTU ICMP message. Case 2: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv4 and has the DF bit clear. The implementation SHOULD fragment (before or after encryption per its configuration) and then forward the fragments. It SHOULD NOT send a PMTU ICMP message. Case 3: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv6. The implementation SHOULD discard the packet and send a PMTU ICMP message. (§8.2.1)

## Superseded

No document obsoletes RFC 4301, so its obligations are stated where they were written.
